\binom{n+k}{n}
.

(iii)

The maximum number of nonzero entries on a row of
C
is
1
.

(iv)

A
has rank
M
,
\sigma_{\min}(A)=1
, and
-I_{N}\preceq A_{i}\preceq I_{N}
, for
i=1,\dots,M

(v)

\lambda^{\star}\in[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
.

(vi)

Problem (
109
) is equivalent to (
50
).

(vii)

\lambda^{\star}=\inf\{\tr(CX)\,:\,X\in\mathbb{S}^{N}_{+}\,,\,\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\}\,.

(264)

Proof.

(
iv
) By Lemma
47
(
i
), the matrices
\check{A}_{1},\dots,\check{A}_{M}
are linearly independent in
\mathbb{S}^{N}
.
By Lemma
62
, we have
\tr(A_{i}A_{j})=\delta_{ij}
, which implies
-I_{N}\preceq A_{i}\preceq I_{N}
,
A
has rank
M
, and
\sigma_{\min}(A)=1
.

(
ii
) Since each
A_{i}
is a linear combination of
\check{A}_{j}
’s, it retains the block-diagonal structure of
\check{A}_{j}
.
Recall that
\check{A}_{j}=\diag(B_{g}^{(\gamma_{j})},0)
.
By the block-diagonal structure of
B_{g}^{(\gamma)}
in (
117
), for
i=0,\dots,m
, the
i
-th block of
B_{g}^{(\gamma)}
has size
\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}
.
Thus, each row of
A_{i}
contains no more than
\binom{n+k}{n}
nonzero entries.

(
i
) By Lemma
47
(
ii
), we have
C=\frac{\check{C}}{c_{g}}=\frac{\check{C}}{\|\check{C}\|_{F}}
.
Thus,
-I_{N}\preceq C\preceq I
and by (
261
),

\|\text{vec}(C)\|_{\ell_{1}}=\frac{\|\text{vec}(\check{C})\|_{\ell_{1}}}{c_{g}}=\frac{|g_{0,0}|+\dots+|g_{m,0}|}{c_{g}}=\frac{|g_{0,0}|+\dots+|g_{m,0}|}{c_{g}}\,.

(265)

(
iii
) follows from Lemma
47
(
iii
) and
C=\check{C}/c_{g}
.

(
vi
) By Lemma
46
, problem (
109
) is equivalent to (
259
).
Additionally, by Lemma
62
, we have

\tr(\check{A}_{i}X)=\check{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\Leftrightarrow\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M

(266)

Therefore, problem (
259
) is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{rl}f_{0}-\lambda_{k}=\inf\limits_{X}&\tr(\check{C}X)\\
\text{s.t.}&X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{N}\,,\,\tr(X)=1\,,\\
&\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\,.\end{array}

(267)

Since
C=\check{C}/c_{g}
, this problem can be written as

\begin{array}[]{rl}\frac{f_{0}-\lambda_{k}}{c_{g}}=\inf\limits_{X}&\tr(CX)\\
\text{s.t.}&X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{N}\,,\,\tr(X)=1\,,\\
&\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\,.\end{array}

(268)

This matches the form of problem (
50
), where
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{f_{0}-\lambda_{k}}{c_{g}}
.

(
v
) Since
\lambda_{\min}=\frac{f_{0}-f(a)}{c_{g}}
for
a\in S(g)
, and
\lambda_{\max}=\frac{f_{0}-\underline{\lambda}}{c_{g}}
with
\underline{\lambda}\leq\lambda_{k}\leq f^{\star}\leq f(a)
, we conclude that
\lambda^{\star}\in[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
.

(
vii
) follows from the last statement of Lemma
46
.
∎

Theorem 9
.

Let
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2d}
and
g_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2d_{i}}
, for
i=0,\dots,m
, with
g_{0}=1
.
Suppose
0\in S(g)
, Assumption
2
holds, and Slater’s condition holds for the sum-of-squares relaxation (
109
), i.e., there exists a feasible solution
G_{n,k}\succ 0
for problem (
109
) with condition number
\kappa
.
Let
\lambda_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}
be the value returned by Algorithm
7
.
Then, the following bound holds:

0\leq\lambda_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}-\lambda_{k}\leq\varepsilon\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa)\sqrt{\binom{n+2k}{n}}\ \right]\sum_{i=0}^{m}|g_{i,0}|\,.

(269)

Proof.

Let
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{f_{0}-\lambda_{k}}{c_{g}}
. Since Slater’s condition holds for the sum-of-squares relaxation (
109
), strong duality is satisfied for problems (
109
)-(
110
).
By Lemma
48
(
vii
), strong duality also holds for problem (
264
) and its dual (
42
) .

Define
U_{n,k}=\diag(G_{n,k},\lambda_{\min}(G_{n,k}))
. Since
\lambda_{\min}(G_{n,k})>0
, it follows that
U_{n,k}\succeq 0
and has the same condition number
\kappa
.
Moreover,
\tr(\check{A}_{i}U_{n,k})=\tr(B^{(\gamma_{i})}G_{n,k})=f_{\gamma_{i}}=\check{b}_{i}
and by (
266
),
\tr(A_{i}U_{n,k})=b_{i}
.
This implies
U_{n,k}
is a feasible solution for problem (
264
), and thus Slater’s condition holds for problem (
264
).
From Lemma
48
(
iv
),
A
has full rank
M
.
Since
0\in S(g)
, we have
\lambda_{k}\leq f^{\star}\leq f(0)=f_{0}
, which implies
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{f_{0}-\lambda_{k}}{c_{g}}\geq 0
.
By Lemma
48
(
i
), (
iv
) and (
v
),
I_{N}\succeq C\succeq-I_{N}
,
I_{N}\succeq A_{i}\succeq-I_{N}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
, and
\lambda^{\star}\in[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
.
Using Lemma
44
(ii), we obtain

0\leq\lambda^{\star}-\overline{\lambda}_{T}\leq\varepsilon\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa)\|\text{vec}(C)\|_{\ell_{1}}\sigma_{\min}(A)^{-1}\sqrt{M}\right]\,.

(270)

Applying properties of
C
and
A
from Lemma
48
(
i
) and (
iv
), this simplifies to

0\leq\lambda^{\star}-\overline{\lambda}_{T}\leq\varepsilon\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa)\frac{b_{g}}{c_{g}}\sqrt{M}\right]\,,

(271)

where
b_{g}=|g_{0,0}|+\dots+|g_{m,0}|
.
Since
\lambda_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}=f_{0}-\underline{\lambda}_{T}c_{g}
and
\lambda_{k}=f_{0}-\lambda^{\star}c_{g}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}0\leq\lambda_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}-\lambda_{k}=c_{g}(\lambda^{\star}-\underline{\lambda}_{T})\leq&\varepsilon\left[2c_{g}+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa)b_{g}\sqrt{M}\right]\\
\leq&\varepsilon b_{g}\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa)\sqrt{M}\right]\,.\end{array}

(272)

The last inequality is due to
c_{g}\leq b_{g}
.
Finally, substituting
M=\binom{n+2k}{n}-1
completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 49

(Complexity)
.

Let
k\geq\max\{d,d_{0},\dots,d_{m}\}
.
To run Algorithm
7
, the classical computer requires

O\left(\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}^{2}+\left[\binom{n+k}{n}\binom{n+2k}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}+\left(\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}\right)^{\omega}\right]\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)

(273)

operations, where
\omega\approx 2.373
, while the quantum computer requires

\footnotesize O\left(\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}^{2}+\binom{n+k}{n}\left[\binom{n+2k}{n}^{1/2}+\left(\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}\right)^{1/2}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right]\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\right)

(274)

operations.

Proof.

The most computationally expensive parts of Algorithm
7
are the Gram-Schmidt process in Step
1d
and the binary search using Hamiltonian updates in Step
2
.

For the Gram-Schmidt process, by Lemma
63
, both classical and quantum computers have the same complexity of
O(LM^{2})
, where

L=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}\left[\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}+1\right]+1\,.

(275)

For the binary search using Hamiltonian updates, by Lemma
15
, the classical computer requires
O((MsN+N^{\omega})\varepsilon^{-2})
operations, and the quantum computer requires
O(s(\sqrt{M}+\sqrt{N}\varepsilon^{-1})\varepsilon^{-4})
operations,
where
s
is the maximum number of nonzero entries in a row of the data matrices
C
and
A_{i}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
.
By Lemma
48
(
ii
) and (
iii
), we have
s=\binom{n+k}{n}
.

Here,
M=\binom{n+2k}{n}-1
and
N=\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}+1
.
Hence the result follows.
∎

B.2
Quantum algorithms for solving moment relaxations

B.2.1
Equality-constrained polynomial optimization over the unit sphere

Let
f,h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]
, for
j=1,\dots,l
, with
h_{1}=1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
.
Consider polynomial optimization problem

f^{\star}=\min\limits_{x\in V(h)}f(x)\,,

(276)

where
V(h)
is the algebraic variety defined as:

V(h)=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,:\,h_{j}(x)=0\,,\,j=1,\dots,l\}\,.

(277)

Define
d=\lceil\deg(f)/2\rceil
and
w_{j}=\lceil\deg(h_{j})/2\rceil
,
j=1,\dots,l
, noting that
w_{1}=1
.

The moment relaxation of order
k
for for this problem is:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau_{k}=\min\limits_{y}&L_{y}(f)\\
\text{s.t.}&y=(y_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}\,,\\
&M_{k}(y)\succeq 0\,,\,y_{0}=1\,,\\
&M_{k-w_{j}}(h_{j}y)=0\,,\,j=1,\dots,l\,,\end{array}

(278)

where
L_{y}
is the Riez linear functional,
M_{k}(y)
denotes the moment matrix, and
M_{k-w_{j}}(h_{j}y)
represents the localizing matrix.

The relaxation sequence satisfies:

\tau_{k}\leq\tau_{k+1}\leq f^{\star}
and
\tau_{k}\to f^{\star}
as
k\to\infty
.

Assume that problem (
278
) has an optimal solution and that a lower bound
\underline{\tau}\leq\tau_{k}
is known.

Algorithm 8
.

Equality constrained polynomial optimization over the unit sphere

•

Input:
\varepsilon>0
, ,
\underline{\tau}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
a\in V(h)
,

1.1cm
coefficients
(f_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}
of
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]
,

1.1cm
coefficients
(h_{j,\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}
of
h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]
, for
j=1,\dots,l
.

•

Output:
\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
Z_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{S}^{N}
.

1.

Convert relaxation to standard semidefinite program:

(a)

Set
u_{\alpha}=\binom{k}{(k-|\alpha|,\alpha)}
,
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
.

(b)

For
\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
, let
\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
be such that
\alpha_{\gamma}\leq\beta_{\gamma}
and
\alpha_{\gamma}+\beta_{\gamma}=\gamma
.

(c)

For
\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
satisfying
\alpha\leq\beta
,
(\alpha,\beta)\neq(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})
, and
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
, let
\tilde{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}=(\tilde{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}_{\xi,\zeta})_{\xi,\zeta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
be the matrix defined by

\tilde{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}_{\xi,\zeta}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{u_{\xi}^{1/2}u_{\zeta}^{1/2}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)=(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})\,,\,\alpha_{\gamma}=\beta_{\gamma}\,,\\
\frac{1}{2u_{\xi}^{1/2}u_{\zeta}^{1/2}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\{(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}),(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})\}\,,\,\alpha_{\gamma}\neq\beta_{\gamma}\,,\\
-\frac{1}{u_{\xi}^{1/2}u_{\zeta}^{1/2}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)=(\alpha,\beta)\,,\,\alpha=\beta\,,\\
-\frac{1}{2u_{\xi}^{1/2}u_{\zeta}^{1/2}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\{(\alpha,\beta),(\alpha,\beta)\}\,,\,\alpha\neq\beta\,,\\
0&\text{else}.\end{cases}

(279)

(d)

For
j=1,\dots,l
, for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}
, let
\tilde{A}^{(j,\alpha)}=(\tilde{A}^{(j,\alpha)}_{\xi,\zeta})_{\xi,\zeta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
be the matrix defined by

\tilde{A}^{(j,\alpha)}_{\xi,\zeta}=\begin{cases}\frac{h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}}{2u_{\xi}^{1/2}u_{\zeta}^{1/2}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\left\{(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}),(\beta_{\gamma},\alpha_{\gamma})\,:\,\alpha_{\gamma}\neq\beta_{\gamma}\,,\right.\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\left.\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}\right\}\,,\\
\frac{h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}}{u_{\xi}^{1/2}u_{\zeta}^{1/2}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\{(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}),\,:\,\alpha_{\gamma}=\beta_{\gamma}\,,\,\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}\}\,,\\
0&\text{else.}\end{cases}

(280)

(e)

Let
\tilde{A}^{(0)}=(\tilde{A}^{(0)}_{\alpha,\beta})_{\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
be the matrix defined by

\tilde{A}^{(0)}_{\alpha,\beta}=\begin{cases}2^{k}&\text{if }\alpha=\beta=0\,,\\
0&\text{else}.\end{cases}

(281)

(f)

Let
\tilde{C}=(\tilde{C}_{\alpha,\beta})_{\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
be the matrix defined by

\tilde{C}_{\alpha,\beta}=\begin{cases}\frac{f_{\gamma}2^{k}}{u_{\alpha}^{1/2}u_{\beta}^{1/2}}&\text{if }(\alpha,\beta)=(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})\,,\,\alpha_{\gamma}=\beta_{\gamma}\,,\\
\frac{f_{\gamma}2^{k-1}}{u_{\alpha}^{1/2}u_{\beta}^{1/2}}&\text{if }(\alpha,\beta)\in\{(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}),(\beta_{\gamma},\alpha_{\gamma})\}\,,\,\alpha_{\gamma}\neq\beta_{\gamma}\,,\\
0&\text{else}.\end{cases}

(282)

(g)

Set
N=\binom{n+k}{n}
.

(h)

Let
\tilde{A}_{1},\dots,\tilde{A}_{\tilde{M}}
be the following matrices:

i.

\tilde{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}
, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
satisfying
\alpha\leq\beta
,
(\alpha,\beta)\neq(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})
, and
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
, for
\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
;

ii.

\tilde{A}^{(j,\alpha)}
, for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}
, for
j=1,\dots,l
;

iii.

\tilde{A}^{(0)}
.

In particular,
\tilde{A}_{\tilde{M}}=\tilde{A}^{(0)}
.

(i)

Let
\tilde{b}_{1},\dots,\tilde{b}_{\tilde{M}}
be defined by
\tilde{b}_{i}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }i=\tilde{M}\,,\\
0&\text{else}.\end{cases}

(j)

Run Algorithm
10
(Gaussian elimination) to get
\check{A}_{i}\in\mathbb{S}^{N}
,
\check{b}_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
i=1,\dots,M
.

(k)

Run Algorithm
11
(Gram-Schmidt process) to get
A_{i}\in\mathbb{S}^{N}
and
b_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
i=1,\dots,M
.

(l)

Set
c=\|\tilde{C}\|_{F}
,
C=\frac{\tilde{C}}{c}
,
\lambda_{\max}=\frac{f(a)}{c}
and
\lambda_{\min}=\frac{\underline{\tau}}{c}
.

2.

Run Algorithm
2
(Binary search using Hamiltonian updates)
to obtain
\underline{\lambda}_{T}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
X_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{S}^{N}
.

3.

Extract approximate results: Set
\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}=\underline{\lambda}_{T}c
and
Z_{\varepsilon}=X_{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 50
.

Let
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2k}
and
h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2w_{j}}
for
j=1,\dots,l
, with
h_{1}=1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
.
Let
u_{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
be the coefficients generated by Algorithm
8
.
Define

U=\diag\left(\left(\frac{u_{\alpha}^{1/2}}{2^{k/2}}\right)_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}\right)\,.

(283)

Then, for any feasible solution
y
to problem (
278
),
\tr(UM_{k}(y)U)=1
.

Proof.

Using the identity
a^{k}-b^{k}=(a-b)\sum_{j=0}^{k}a^{k-1-j}b^{j}
with
a=2
and
b=1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
, we derive

2^{k}=(1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})^{k}+(1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})p\,,

(284)

where
p=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2^{k-1-j}(1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})^{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2(k-1)}
.
For
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})
and
\bar{\alpha}=(\alpha_{0},\alpha)
, we expand
(1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})^{k}
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rl}(1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})^{k}=&(1+x_{1}^{2}+\dots+x_{n}^{2})^{k}\\[10.0pt]
=&\sum\limits_{\begin{array}[]{cc}\scriptstyle\bar{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n+1}\\
\scriptstyle|\bar{\alpha}|=k\end{array}}\binom{k}{\bar{\alpha}}1^{\alpha_{0}}x_{1}^{2\alpha_{1}}\dots x_{n}^{2\alpha_{n}}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}\binom{k}{(k-|\alpha|,\alpha)}x^{2\alpha}\,.\end{array}

(285)

With
u_{\alpha}=\binom{k}{(k-|\alpha|,\alpha)}
, , it follows that
(1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})^{k}=\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}x^{2\alpha}
.
Substituting this back into the equality (
284
), we obtain:

2^{k}=\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}x^{2\alpha}+h_{1}p

(286)

Now let
y
be a feasible solution to problem (
278
).
Applying the linear operator
L_{y}
for both sides, we get:

L_{y}(2^{k})=L_{y}\left(\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}x^{2\alpha}\right)+L_{y}(h_{1}p)\,.

(287)

Since
L_{y}(2^{k})=2^{k}y_{0}=2^{k}
, and
L_{y}(\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}x^{2\alpha})=\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}y_{2\alpha}
, it remains to evaluate
L_{y}(h_{1}p)
.
Note that
L_{y}(h_{1}p)=0
because
M_{k-w_{j}}(h_{j}y)=0
and
\deg(p)=2(k-u_{1})
.
Thus,
2^{k}=\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}y_{2\alpha}
.
Finally, by the definition of
U
,

\tr(UM_{k}(y)U)=\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}\left(\frac{u_{\alpha}^{1/2}}{2^{k/2}}\right)^{2}y_{2\alpha}=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}y_{2\alpha}\,.

(288)

Since
2^{k}=\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}y_{2\alpha}
, it follows that
\tr(UM_{k}(y)U)=1
, completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 51
.

Let
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2k}
and
h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2w_{j}}
for
j=1,\dots,l
, with
h_{1}=1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
.
Let
\tilde{C}
,
\tilde{A}_{i}
,
\tilde{b}_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}
,
N
be generated by Algorithm
8
.
Then:

(i)

Problem (
278
) is equivalent to the SDP

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau_{k}=\inf\limits_{Z}&\tr(\tilde{C}Z)\\
\text{s.t.}&Z\in\mathbb{S}^{N}_{+}\,,\,\tr(Z)=1\,,\\
&\tr(\tilde{A}_{i}Z)=\tilde{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}\,.\end{array}

(289)

(ii)

The constraint
tr(Z)=1
in the above SDP can be omitted without changing the optimal value:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau_{k}=\inf\limits_{Z}&\tr(\tilde{C}Z)\\
\text{s.t.}&Z\in\mathbb{S}^{N}_{+}\,,\\
&\tr(\tilde{A}_{i}Z)=\tilde{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}\,.\end{array}

(290)

Moreover, if
y
is a feasible solution for problem (
278
), then
UM_{k}(y)U
is a feasible solution for (
290
), where
U
is defined as in (
283
).

(iii)

\|\tilde{C}\|_{F}\leq 2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{2}}
and
\|\text{vec}(\tilde{C})\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq 2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}
.

(iv)

If
k\geq\max\{w_{1},\dots,w_{l}\}
, then the total number of constraints
\tilde{M}
is

\tilde{M}=1+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\binom{n+2(k-w_{j})}{n}+\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}+1\right]-\binom{n+2k}{n}\,,

(291)

and it satisfies:

\tilde{M}\leq 1+(l-1)\binom{n+2k}{n}+\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}+1\right]\,.

(292)

Proof.

(
i
) Let
y
be a feasible solution to problem (
278
).
Define
Z=UM_{k}(y)U
, where
U
is constructed as in Lemma
50
. For
Z=(z_{\alpha,\beta})_{\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
, the trace condition from Lemma
50
ensures
\tr(Z)=1
.
For
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
, using
z_{\alpha,\beta}=\frac{1}{2^{k}}u_{\alpha}^{1/2}u_{\beta}^{1/2}y_{\alpha+\beta}
, we have
y_{\alpha+\beta}=2^{k}\frac{z_{\alpha,\beta}}{u_{\alpha}^{1/2}u_{\beta}^{1/2}}
.
Recall that for
\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
,
\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
satisfy
\alpha_{\gamma}\leq\beta_{\gamma}
and
\alpha_{\gamma}+\beta_{\gamma}=\gamma
.
Thus,
y_{\gamma}=2^{k}\frac{z_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}
.
Additionaly, for all
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
satisfying
\alpha\leq\beta
,
(\alpha,\beta)\neq(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})
, and
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
,

2^{k}\frac{z_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}=y_{\gamma}=y_{\alpha+\beta}=2^{k}\frac{z_{\alpha,\beta}}{u_{\alpha}^{1/2}u_{\beta}^{1/2}}\,,

(293)

which gives

\frac{z_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}=\frac{z_{\alpha,\beta}}{u_{\alpha}^{1/2}u_{\beta}^{1/2}}\,.

(294)

With
\tilde{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}
defined as in (
279
), this condition is equivalent to:

\tr(\tilde{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}Z)=0\,.

(295)

Let
y_{\gamma}
exist
l_{\gamma}
times in the upper triangular portion of
M_{k}(y)
.
It implies that there are
l_{\gamma}-1
affine constraints of form (
295
) for each
\gamma
.
Note that the number of entries in the upper triangular portion of
M_{k}(y)
is
\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}l_{\gamma}=\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]
.
Thus, the number of
\tilde{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}
, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
satisfying
\alpha\leq\beta
,
(\alpha,\beta)\neq(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})
, and
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
, for
\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
, is

\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}(l_{\gamma}-1)=\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}+1\right]-\binom{n+2k}{n}\,.

(296)

Next, constraint
M_{k-w_{j}}(h_{j}y)=0
is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{rl}\forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}\,,\,0=&L_{y}(x^{\alpha}h_{j})=L_{y}(x^{\alpha}\sum_{\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}h_{j,\beta}x^{\beta})\\
=&L_{y}(\sum_{\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}h_{j,\beta}x^{\alpha+\beta})\\
=&\sum_{\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}h_{j,\beta}y_{\alpha+\beta}\\
=&\sum_{\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}y_{\gamma}\\
=&\sum_{\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}\left(2^{k}\frac{z_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}\right)\\
=&2^{k}\sum_{\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}\frac{h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}z_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}\,.\end{array}

(297)

Then constraint
M_{k-w_{j}}(h_{j}y)=0
can be reformulated as

\forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}\,,\,\sum_{\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}\frac{h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}z_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}=0\,.

(298)

With
\tilde{A}^{(j,\alpha)}
defined as in (
280
), constraints (
298
) becomes

\tr(\tilde{A}^{(j,\alpha)}Z)=0\,,\,\forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}\,.

(299)

Note that the number of
\tilde{A}^{(j,\alpha)}
, for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}
, for
j=1,\dots,l
, is

\sum_{j=1}^{l}|{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}|=\sum_{j=1}^{l}\binom{n+2(k-w_{j})}{n}\,.

(300)

The last constrain
y_{0}=1
translates into affine constraints of the form

1=y_{0}=\frac{2^{k}z_{0,0}}{u_{0}^{1/2}u_{0}^{1/2}}=2^{k}z_{0,0}=\tr(\tilde{A}^{(0)}Z)\,,

(301)

where
\tilde{A}^{(0)}
is defined as in (
281
).
The objective function of (
278
) can be written as

L_{y}(f)=\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}f_{\gamma}y_{\gamma}=\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}f_{\gamma}2^{k}\frac{z_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}=\tr(\tilde{C}Z)\,,

(302)

where
\tilde{C}=(\tilde{C}_{\alpha,\beta})_{\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
is the matrix defined as in (
282
).
Thus, (
278
) is equivalent to (
289
).

(
iii
) From the definition of
\tilde{C}
, we have

\|\tilde{C}\|_{F}\leq\max_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}\frac{2^{k}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq 2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\,,

(303)

\|\text{vec}(\tilde{C})\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq\max_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}\frac{2^{k}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq 2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\,.

(304)

(
ii
) Since removing the trace constraint
\tr(G)=1
from (
289
) does not affect the optimal value, the same reasoning leads directly to the formulation in (
290
).

(
iv
) Recall that the total number of affine constraints in the SDP (
289
) includes:

•

Constraints due to the structure of the moment matrix
M_{k}(y)
, which contribute

\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}+1\right]-\binom{n+2k}{n}\,.

(305)

•

Constraints arising from
M_{k-w_{j}}(h_{j}y)=0
, for
j=1,\dots,l
, which contribute

\sum_{j=1}^{l}\binom{n+2(k-w_{j})}{n}\,.

(306)

•

A single constraint from
y_{0}=1
.

Summing these contributions gives the total number of affine constraints
\tilde{M}
. From this, the stated bound on
\tilde{M}
follows.
∎

Remark 12
.

The maximum number of nonzero entries in a row of

\tilde{C},\tilde{A}_{i}
is bounded by

\begin{array}[]{rl}\max\limits_{j=1,\dots,l}\{|\supp(f)|,|\supp(h_{j})|\}\leq&\max\limits_{j=1,\dots,l}\{|{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d}|,|{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}|\}\\[5.0pt]
=&\max\limits_{j=1,\dots,l}\left\{\binom{n+2d}{n},\binom{n+2w_{j}}{n}\right\}\,.\end{array}

(307)

However, due to Gaussian elimination and the Gram-Schmidt process, each
A_{j}
can be expressed as a linear combination of the
\tilde{A}_{i}
’s, causing this sparsity structure to be disrupted.

Lemma 52
.

Let
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2k}
and
h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2w_{j}}
, for
j=1,\dots,l
, with
h_{1}=1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
.
Assume
k\geq\max\{w_{1},\dots,w_{l}\}
.
Let
C
,
A_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,M
,
N
,
c
,
\lambda_{\min}
,
\lambda_{\max}
be generated by Algorithm
8
.
Define

A=\begin{bmatrix}\text{vec}(A_{1})\\
\dots\\
\text{vec}(A_{M})\end{bmatrix}\,.

(308)

Let
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{\tau_{k}}{c}
.
The following properties hold:

(i)

-I_{N}\preceq C\preceq I_{N}
and
\|\text{vec}(C)\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq\frac{2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}}{c}
.

(ii)

A
has rank
M
,
\sigma_{\min}(A)=1
, and
-I_{N}\preceq A_{i}\preceq I_{N}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
.

(iii)

\lambda^{\star}\in[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
.

(iv)

Problem (
278
) is equivalent to (
50
).

(v)

c\leq 2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{2}}
.

(vi)

M\leq\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]
and
N=\binom{n+k}{n}
.

(vii)

\lambda^{\star}=\inf\{\tr(CX)\,:\,X\in\mathbb{S}^{N}_{+}\,,\,\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\}\,.

(309)

Moreover, if
y
is a feasible solution for problem (
278
), then
UM_{k}(y)U
is a feasible solution for (
309
), where
U
is defined as in (
283
).

Proof.

(
iv
) By Lemma
60
, the matrices
\check{A}_{1},\dots,\check{A}_{M}
are linearly independent in
\mathbb{S}^{N}
and

\tr(\tilde{A}_{i}Z)=\tilde{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}\Leftrightarrow\tr(\check{A}_{i}Z)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\,.

(310)

By Lemma
62
, we have
\tr(A_{i}A_{j})=\delta_{ij}
and

\tr(\check{A}_{i}X)=\check{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\Leftrightarrow\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M

(311)

Thus, the problem (
289
) is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau_{k}=\inf\limits_{Z}&\tr(\tilde{C}Z)\\
\text{s.t.}&Z\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{N}\,,\,\tr(Z)=1\,,\\
&\tr(A_{i}Z)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\,.\end{array}

(312)

Since
C=\frac{\tilde{C}}{c}
, this problem can be written as

\begin{array}[]{rl}\frac{\tau_{k}}{c}=\inf\limits_{Z}&\tr(CZ)\\
\text{s.t.}&Z\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{N}\,,\,\tr(Z)=1\,,\\
&\tr(A_{i}Z)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\,.\end{array}

(313)

With
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{\tau_{k}}{c}
, this is equivalent to (
50
).

(
vii
) follows from Lemma
51
(
ii
).

(
ii
) Since
\tr(A_{i}A_{j})=\delta_{ij}
,
-I_{N}\preceq A_{i}\preceq I_{N}
,
A
has rank
M
and
\sigma_{\min}(A)=1
.

(
v
) follows from
c=\|\tilde{C}\|_{F}
and Lemma
51
(
iii
).

(
i
) Since
C=\frac{\tilde{C}}{c}=\frac{\tilde{C}}{\|\tilde{C}\|_{F}}
, we have
-I_{N}\preceq C\preceq I
and
\|\text{vec}(C)\|_{\ell_{1}}=\frac{\|\text{vec}(\tilde{C})\|_{\ell_{1}}}{c}\leq\frac{2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}}{c}
.

(
iii
) Since
\lambda_{\max}=\frac{f(a)}{c}
for
a\in V(h)
,
\lambda_{\min}=\frac{\underline{\tau}}{c}
and
\underline{\tau}\leq\tau_{k}\leq f^{\star}\leq f(a)
, we obtain
\lambda^{\star}\in[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
.

(
vi
) follows from
M\leq\min\{\tilde{M},L\}
, with
L=\frac{N(N+1)}{2}
, and Lemma
51
(
iv
).
∎

Theorem 10
.

Let
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2k}
and
h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2w_{j}}
, for
j=1,\dots,l
, with
h_{1}=1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
.
Assume
k\geq\max\{d,w_{1},\dots,w_{l}\}
,
\tau_{k}\geq 0
, and Slater’s condition holds for the moment relaxation (
278
), i.e., there exists a feasible solution
\bar{y}
for problem (
278
) such that
M_{k}(\bar{y})\succ 0
and has condition number
\kappa
.
Let
\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}
be the value returned by Algorithm
8
.
Then, the following inequality holds:

\begin{array}[]{rl}0\leq&\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}-\tau_{k}\\
\leq&\varepsilon 2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\left[2+\left(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa\max\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}\binom{k}{(k-|\alpha|,\alpha)}\right)\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}+1\right]}\ \right]\,.\end{array}

(314)

Proof.

Let
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{\tau_{k}}{c}
. Since Slater’s condition holds for the moment relaxation (
278
), strong duality is satisfied for problem (
278
) and its dual.
By Lemma
52
(
vii
), strong duality also holds for problem (
264
) and its dual (
42
).

Define
U_{n,k}=UM_{k}(\bar{y})U
, where
U
is defined as in (
283
). Since
U\succ 0
, it follows that
U_{n,k}\succ 0
and has condition number

\kappa(U_{n,k})\leq\kappa\max\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}\,.

(315)

By Lemma
52
(
vii
),
U_{n,k}
is a feasible solution for problem (
309
), and thus Slater’s condition holds for problem (
309
).
From Lemma
52
(
ii
),
A
has full rank
M
.
Since
\tau_{k}\geq 0
, we have
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{\tau_{k}}{c}\geq 0
.
By Lemma
52
(
i
), (
ii
) and (
iii
),
I_{N}\succeq C\succeq-I_{N}
,
I_{N}\succeq A_{i}\succeq-I_{N}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
, and
\lambda^{\star}\in[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
.
Using Lemma
44
(ii), we obtain

0\leq\lambda^{\star}-\overline{\lambda}_{T}\leq\varepsilon\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa(U_{n,k}))\|\text{vec}(C)\|_{\ell_{1}}\sigma_{\min}(A)^{-1}\sqrt{M}\right]\,.

(316)

Applying properties of
C
and
A
from Lemma
52
(
i
) and (
ii
), this simplifies to

0\leq\lambda^{\star}-\overline{\lambda}_{T}\leq\varepsilon\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa\max\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha})\frac{2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}}{c}\sqrt{M}\right]\,.

(317)

Since
\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}=\underline{\lambda}_{T}c
and
\tau_{k}=\lambda^{\star}c
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}0\leq\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}-\tau_{k}=&c(\lambda^{\star}-\underline{\lambda}_{T})\\[5.0pt]
\leq&\varepsilon\left[2c+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa\max\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha})2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\sqrt{M}\right]\\[10.0pt]
\leq&\varepsilon\left[2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{2}}+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa\max\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha})2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\sqrt{M}\right]\\[10.0pt]
\leq&\varepsilon 2^{k}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa\max\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha})\sqrt{M}\right]\,.\end{array}

(318)

The third inequality is due to Lemma
52
(
v
).
Finally, using equality
u_{\alpha}=\binom{k}{(k-|\alpha|,\alpha)}
, for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
, and inequality
M\leq\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]
completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 53

(Complexity)
.

Assume
k\geq\max\{d,w_{1},\dots,w_{l}\}
.
To run Algorithm
8
, the time complexity on a classical computer is

O\left(\binom{n+k}{n}^{4}\left[(l-1)\binom{n+2k}{n}+\binom{n+k}{n}^{2}\right]+\binom{n+k}{n}^{4}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\,,

(319)

while on a quantum computer, the time complexity is

\footnotesize O\left(\binom{n+k}{n}^{1.5}\left\{(l-1)\binom{n+2k}{n}\binom{n+k}{n}^{2.5}+\binom{n+k}{n}^{4.5}+\left[\binom{n+k}{n}^{0.5}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right]\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\right\}\right)\,.

(320)

Proof.

The most computationally expensive steps in Algorithm
8
are performing Gaussian elimination in Step
1j
, the Gram-Schmidt process in Step
1k
, and running binary search using Hamiltonian updates in Step
2
.

For Gaussian elimination, by Lemma
61
, both classical and quantum computers have the same complexity:
O(\tilde{M}N^{4})
.

For the Gram-Schmidt process, by Lemma
63
, both classical and quantum computers have the same complexity:
O(N^{2}M^{2})
.

For binary search using Hamiltonian updates, by Lemma
15
, a classical computer takes
O((MsN+N^{\omega})\varepsilon^{-2})
, a classical computer takes
O(s(\sqrt{M}+\sqrt{N}\varepsilon^{-1})\varepsilon^{-4})
times on a quantum computer,
, where
s
is the maximum number of nonzero entries in a row of the data matrices
C,A_{i}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
.
We take
s=N
, which is the size of
C,A_{i}
.

By Lemma
52
(
vi
), we have
M\leq\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]
,
N=\binom{n+k}{n}
.
Furthermore, by Lemma
51
(
iv
),

\tilde{M}\leq 1+(l-1)\binom{n+2k}{n}+\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]
.
Thus, the result follows.
∎

B.2.2
(In)equality-constrained polynomial optimization over the unit ball

Let
f,g_{i},h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]
for
i=1,\dots,m
and
j=1,\dots,l
, with
g_{1}=1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
.
Consider polynomial optimization problem

f^{\star}=\min\limits_{x\in S(g)\cap V(h)}f(x)\,,

(321)

where
S(g)
s the semialgebraic set defined as in (
107
) and
V(h)
is the algebraic variety defined as in (
277
).

Define
d=\lceil\deg(f)/2\rceil
,
d_{i}=\lceil\deg(g_{i})/2\rceil
, for
i=1,\dots,m
, and
w_{j}=\lceil\deg(h_{j})/2\rceil
, for
j=1,\dots,l
.
Here,
d_{1}=1
.

The moment relaxation of order
k
for problem (
321
) is expressed as:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau_{k}=\min\limits_{y}&L_{y}(f)\\
\text{s.t.}&y=(y_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}\,,\\
&M_{k}(y)\succeq 0\,,\,y_{0}=1\,,\\
&M_{k-d_{i}}(g_{i}y)\succeq 0\,,\,i=1,\dots,m\,,\\
&M_{k-w_{j}}(h_{j}y)=0\,,\,j=1,\dots,l\,.\end{array}

(322)

For this relaxation, we have the following properties:

\tau_{k}\leq\tau_{k+1}\leq f^{\star}
and
\tau_{k}\to f^{\star}
as
k\to\infty
.

Assume that problem (
322
) has an optimal solution and that a lower bound
\underline{\tau}\leq\tau_{k}
is known.

Algorithm 9
.

(In)equality constrained polynomial optimization over the unit ball

•

Input:
\varepsilon>0
,
\underline{\tau}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
a\in S(g)\cap V(h)
,

1.1cm
coefficients
(f_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}
of
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]
,

1.1cm
coefficients
(g_{i,\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}}
of
g_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]
, for
i=0,\dots,m
,

1.1cm
coefficients
(h_{j,\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}
of
h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]
, for
j=1,\dots,l
.

•

Output:
\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
Z_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{S}^{\bar{N}}
.

1.

Convert relaxation to standard SDP:

(a)

Define
u_{\alpha}=\binom{k}{(k-|\alpha|,\alpha)}
, for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
, and
p_{\alpha}=\sum_{j=|\alpha|}^{k-1}2^{k-1-j}\binom{j}{(j-|\alpha|,\alpha)}
, for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-1}
.

(b)

Let
W\in\mathbb{S}^{\binom{n+k}{n}}
satisfy

\sum_{i=2}^{m}g_{i}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}x^{2\alpha}=v_{k}^{\top}Wv_{k}\,.

(323)

(c)

Let

\delta=\frac{\min_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}}{2\sigma_{\max}(W)+1}\,.

(324)

(d)

Set

\begin{array}[]{rl}&p_{\alpha}^{(0)}=\frac{u_{\alpha}^{1/2}}{2^{(k+1)/2}}\,,\,\forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}\,,\\[5.0pt]
&p_{\alpha}^{(1)}=\frac{p_{\alpha}^{1/2}}{2^{k/2}}\,,\,\forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{1}}\,,\\[5.0pt]
&p_{\alpha}^{(i)}=\delta^{1/2}\,,\,\forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}\,,\,i=2,\dots,m\,.\end{array}

(325)

(e)

For
\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
, let
\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
be such that
\alpha_{\gamma}\leq\beta_{\gamma}
and
\alpha_{\gamma}+\beta_{\gamma}=\gamma
.

(f)

For
\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
satisfying
\alpha\leq\beta
,
(\alpha,\beta)\neq(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})
, and
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
, let
\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,0)}=(\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,0)}_{\xi,\zeta})_{\xi,\zeta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
be the matrix defined by

\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,0)}_{\xi,\zeta}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{p_{\xi}^{(0)}p_{\zeta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)=(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})\,,\,\alpha_{\gamma}=\beta_{\gamma}\,,\\
\frac{1}{2p_{\xi}^{(0)}p_{\zeta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\{(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}),(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})\}\,,\,\alpha_{\gamma}\neq\beta_{\gamma}\,,\\
-\frac{1}{p_{\xi}^{(0)}p_{\zeta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)=(\alpha,\beta)\,,\,\alpha=\beta\,,\\
-\frac{1}{2p_{\xi}^{(0)}p_{\zeta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\{(\alpha,\beta),(\alpha,\beta)\}\,,\,\alpha\neq\beta\,,\\
0&\text{else},\end{cases}

(326)

and set
\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}=\diag(\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,0)},0,\dots,0)
.

(g)

For
i=1,\dots,m
, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}
,
\alpha\leq\beta
, let
\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,0)}=(\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,0)}_{\xi,\zeta})_{\xi,\zeta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
be the matrix defined by

\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,0)}_{\xi,\zeta}=\begin{cases}\frac{g_{i,\gamma-\alpha-\beta}}{2p_{\xi}^{(0)}p_{\zeta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\left\{(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}),(\beta_{\gamma},\alpha_{\gamma})\,:\,\alpha_{\gamma}\neq\beta_{\gamma}\,,\right.\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\gamma\in\alpha+\beta+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}\right\}\,,\\
\frac{g_{i,\gamma-\alpha-\beta}}{p_{\xi}^{(0)}p_{\zeta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\left\{(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}),\,:\,\alpha_{\gamma}=\beta_{\gamma}\,,\right.\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\gamma\in\alpha+\beta+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}\right\}\,,\\
0&\text{else,}\end{cases}

(327)

let
\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,i)}=(\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,i)}_{\xi,\zeta})_{\xi,\zeta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}
be the matrix defined by

\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,i)}_{\xi,\zeta}=\begin{cases}-\frac{1}{2p_{\xi}^{(0)}p_{\zeta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\{(\alpha,\beta),(\beta,\alpha)\}\,,\,\alpha\neq\beta\,,\\
-\frac{1}{p_{\xi}^{(0)}p_{\zeta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)=(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})\,,\,\alpha=\beta\,,\\
0&\text{else,}\end{cases}

(328)

and set
\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta)}=\diag(\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,0)},0,\dots,0,\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,i)},0,\dots,0)
.

(h)

For
j=1,\dots,l
, for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}
, let
\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha,0)}=(\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha,0)}_{\xi,\zeta})_{\xi,\zeta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
be the matrix defined by

\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha,0)}_{\xi,\zeta}=\begin{cases}\frac{h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}}{2p_{\xi}^{(0)}p_{\zeta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\left\{(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}),(\beta_{\gamma},\alpha_{\gamma})\,:\,\alpha_{\gamma}\neq\beta_{\gamma}\,,\right.\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}\right\}\,,\\
\frac{h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}}{p_{\xi}^{(0)}p_{\zeta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\xi,\zeta)\in\{(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}),\,:\,\alpha_{\gamma}=\beta_{\gamma}\,,\,\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}\}\,,\\
0&\text{else,}\end{cases}

(329)

and set
\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha)}=\diag(\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha,0)},0,\dots,0)
.

(i)

Let
\bar{A}^{(0,0)}=(\bar{A}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(0,0)})_{\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
be the matrix defined by

\bar{A}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(0,0)}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{p_{0}^{(0)2}}&\text{if }\alpha=\beta=0\,,\\
0&\text{else},\end{cases}

(330)

and set
\bar{A}^{(0)}=\diag(\bar{A}^{(0,0)},0,\dots,0)
.

(j)

Let
\bar{C}^{(0)}=(\bar{C}^{(0)}_{\alpha,\beta})_{\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
be the matrix defined by

\bar{C}^{(0)}_{\alpha,\beta}=\begin{cases}\frac{f_{\gamma}}{p_{\alpha}^{(0)}p_{\beta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\alpha,\beta)=(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})\,,\,\alpha_{\gamma}=\beta_{\gamma}\,,\\
\frac{f_{\gamma}}{2p_{\alpha}^{(0)}p_{\beta}^{(0)}}&\text{if }(\alpha,\beta)\in\{(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}),(\beta_{\gamma},\alpha_{\gamma})\}\,,\,\alpha_{\gamma}\neq\beta_{\gamma}\,,\\
0&\text{else},\end{cases}

(331)

and set
\bar{C}=\diag(\bar{C}^{(0)},0,\dots,0)
.

(k)

Set
\bar{N}=\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}
.

(l)

Let
\bar{A}_{1},\dots,\bar{A}_{\tilde{M}}
be the following matrices:

i.

\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}
, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
satisfying
\alpha\leq\beta
,
(\alpha,\beta)\neq(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})
, and
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
, for
\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
;

ii.

\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta)}
, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i})}
, for
i=1,\dots,m
;

iii.

\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha)}
, for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}
, for
j=1,\dots,l
;

iv.

\bar{A}^{(0)}
.

In particular,
\bar{A}_{\tilde{M}}=\bar{A}^{(0)}
.

(m)

Let
\bar{b}_{1},\dots,\bar{b}_{\tilde{M}}
be defined by
\bar{b}_{i}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }i=\tilde{M}\,,\\
0&\text{else}.\end{cases}

(n)

Set
\tilde{C}=\diag(\bar{C},0)
,
\tilde{A}_{i}=\diag(\bar{A}_{i},0)
,
\tilde{b}_{i}=\bar{b}_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}
,
N=\bar{N}+1
.

(o)

Run Algorithm
10
(Gaussian elimination) to get
\check{A}_{i}\in\mathbb{S}^{N}
,
\check{b}_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
i=1,\dots,M
.

(p)

Run Algorithm
11
(Gram-Schmidt process) to get
A_{i}\in\mathbb{S}^{N}
and
b_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
i=1,\dots,M
.

(q)

Set
c=\|\tilde{C}\|_{F}
,
C=\frac{\tilde{C}}{c}
,
\lambda_{\max}=\frac{f(a)}{c}
and
\lambda_{\min}=\frac{\underline{\tau}}{c}
.

2.

Run Algorithm
2
(Binary search using Hamiltonian updates)
to obtain
\underline{\lambda}_{T}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
X_{\varepsilon}=((X_{\varepsilon})_{ij})_{i,j=1,\dots,N}\in\mathbb{S}^{N}
.

3.

Extract approximate results: Set
\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}=\underline{\lambda}_{T}c
and
Z_{\varepsilon}=((X_{\varepsilon})_{ij})_{i,j=1,\dots,\bar{N}}
.

Lemma 54
.

Let
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2k}
and
g_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2d_{i}}
, for
i=1,\dots,m
,
h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2w_{j}}
, for
j=1,\dots,l
, with
g_{1}=1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
.
Let
p_{\alpha}^{(i)}
, for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}
and
i=0,\dots,m
, be generated by Algorithm
9
, and define
P^{(i)}=\diag(p_{\alpha}^{(i)})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}
, for
i=0,\dots,m
.
Then, for any feasible solution
y
to problem (
322
),

\tr(PD_{k}(gy)P)\leq 1\,,

(332)

where
D_{k}(gy)=\diag((M_{k-d_{i}}(g_{i}y))_{i=0,\dots,m})
and
P=\diag((P^{(i)})_{i=0,\dots,m})
.

Proof.

Using the identity
a^{k}-b^{k}=(a-b)\sum_{j=0}^{k}a^{k-1-j}b^{j}
with
a=2
and
b=1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
, we have

2^{k}=(1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})^{k}+(1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})p\,,

(333)

where
p=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2^{k-1-j}(1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})^{j}
.
For
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})
and
\bar{\alpha}=(\alpha_{0},\alpha)
, we express
(1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})^{k}
in term of monomials:

\begin{array}[]{rl}(1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})^{k}=&(1+x_{1}^{2}+\dots+x_{n}^{2})^{k}\\[5.0pt]
=&\sum\limits_{\begin{array}[]{cc}\scriptstyle\bar{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n+1}\\
\scriptstyle|\bar{\alpha}|=k\end{array}}\binom{k}{\bar{\alpha}}1^{\alpha_{0}}x_{1}^{2\alpha_{1}}\dots x_{n}^{2\alpha_{n}}=\sum\limits_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}\binom{k}{(k-|\alpha|,\alpha)}x^{2\alpha}\,.\end{array}

(334)

With
u_{\alpha}=\binom{k}{(k-|\alpha|,\alpha)}
, we obtan
(1+\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2})^{k}=\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}x^{2\alpha}
.
Similarly,
p
can be written as

\begin{array}[]{rl}p=&\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2^{k-1-j}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{j}}\binom{j}{(j-|\alpha|,\alpha)}x^{2\alpha}\\[10.0pt]
=&\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-1}}\sum_{j=|\alpha|}^{k-1}2^{k-1-j}\binom{j}{(j-|\alpha|,\alpha)}x^{2\alpha}\,.\end{array}

(335)

With
p_{\alpha}=\sum_{j=|\alpha|}^{k-1}2^{k-1-j}\binom{j}{(j-|\alpha|,\alpha)}
, we have
p=\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{1}}}p_{\alpha}x^{2\alpha}
.
Equality (
333
) becomes

2^{k}=\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}x^{2\alpha}+g_{1}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{1}}}p_{\alpha}x^{2\alpha}

(336)

With
\delta>0
, we write

\begin{array}[]{rl}2^{k}=&\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}u_{\alpha}x^{2\alpha}+g_{1}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{1}}}p_{\alpha}x^{2\alpha}+\delta\sum_{i=2}^{m}g_{i}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}x^{2\alpha}\\[10.0pt]
&+v_{k}^{\top}Gv_{k}\,,\end{array}

(337)

where
W\in\mathbb{S}^{\binom{n+k}{n}}
satisfies (
323
) and
G=\frac{1}{2}[\diag((u_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}})-2\delta W]
.
Choosing
\delta
as in (
324
) gives
G\succeq 0
.
Dividing both sides of (
337
) implies

\begin{array}[]{rl}1=&\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}\frac{u_{\alpha}}{2^{k+1}}x^{2\alpha}+g_{1}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{1}}}\frac{p_{\alpha}}{2^{k}}x^{2\alpha}+\delta(\sum_{i=2}^{m}g_{i}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}x^{2\alpha})\\[10.0pt]
&+\frac{1}{2^{k}}v_{k}^{\top}Gv_{k}\\[10.0pt]
=&\sum_{i=0}^{m}g_{i}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}p_{\alpha}^{(i)2}x^{2\alpha}+\frac{1}{2^{k}}v_{k}^{\top}Gv_{k}\,,\end{array}

(338)

where
p_{\alpha}^{(i)}
is defined as in (
325
)
With
P^{(i)}=\diag(p_{\alpha}^{(i)})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}
, we obtain

1=\sum_{i=0}^{m}g_{i}v_{k-d_{i}}^{\top}P^{(i)2}v_{k-d_{i}}+\frac{1}{2^{k}}v_{k}^{\top}Gv_{k}\,.

(339)

Let
y
be a feasible solution of problem (
278
).
Applying
L_{y}
for both sides yields

\begin{array}[]{rl}1=&\sum_{i=0}^{m}\tr(M_{k-d_{i}}(g_{i}y)P^{(i)2})+\frac{1}{2^{k}}\tr(GM_{k}(y))\\[5.0pt]
\geq&\sum_{i=0}^{m}\tr(M_{k-d_{i}}(g_{i}y)P^{(i)2})\\[5.0pt]
=&\sum_{i=0}^{m}\tr(P^{(i)}M_{k-d_{i}}(g_{i}y)P^{(i)})=\tr(PD_{k}(y)P)\,.\end{array}

(340)

The last inequality follows from
G\succeq 0
and
M_{k}(y)\succeq 0
.
Hence, the result is proved.
∎

Lemma 55
.

Let
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2k}
and
g_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2d_{i}}
, for
i=1,\dots,m
,
h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2w_{j}}
, for
j=1,\dots,l
, with
g_{1}=1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
.
Let
\bar{C}
,
\bar{A}_{i}
, for
i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}
,
\bar{N}
,
c
,
\lambda_{\min}
, and
\lambda_{\max}
be generated by Algorithm
9
.
The following properties hold:

(i)

Problem (
322
) is equivalent to the semidefinite program (SDP):

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau_{k}=\min\limits_{Z}&\tr(\bar{C}Z)\\
\text{s.t.}&Z\in\mathbb{S}^{\bar{N}}_{+}\,,\,\tr(Z)\leq 1\,,\\
&\tr(\bar{A}_{i}Z)=\bar{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}\,.\end{array}

(341)

(ii)

\|\bar{C}\|_{F}\leq 2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{2}}
and
\|\text{vec}(\bar{C})\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq 2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}
.

(iii)

If
k\geq\max\{d_{1},\dots,d_{m}\}
, then the size
\bar{N}
is bounded as:
\bar{N}\leq(m+1)\binom{n+k}{n}
.

(iv)

If
k\geq\max\{d_{1},\dots,d_{m},w_{1},\dots,w_{l}\}
, then the total number of constraints
\tilde{M}
is

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tilde{M}=&1+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\binom{n+2(k-w_{j})}{n}+\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]-\binom{n+2k}{n}\\[5.0pt]
&+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}[\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}+1]\,.\end{array}

(342)

(v)

The optimal value of (
341
) is unchanged when the trace constraint is omitted:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau_{k}=\min\limits_{Z}&\tr(\bar{C}Z)\\
\text{s.t.}&Z\in\mathbb{S}^{\bar{N}}_{+}\,,\\
&\tr(\bar{A}_{i}Z)=\bar{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}\,.\end{array}

(343)

Moreover, if
y
is a feasible solution for problem (
322
), then
PD_{k}(gy)P
is a feasible solution for (
343
), where
P
is constructed as in Lemma
54
.

Proof.

(
i
) Let
y
be a feasible solution of problem (
278
).
Define
Z=PD_{k}(y)P
.
Since
D_{k}(y)\in\mathbb{S}^{\bar{N}}_{+}
, it follows that
Z\in\mathbb{S}^{\bar{N}}_{+}
.
Using Lemma
54
, we have
\tr(Z)\leq 1
.
We express
Z
in block-diagonal form as

Z=\diag(Z^{(0)},\dots,Z^{(m)})\,,

(344)

where
Z^{(i)}=(z^{(i)}_{\alpha,\beta})_{\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}
, for
i=0,\dots,m
.
In particular,
Z^{(0)}=P^{(0)}M_{k}(y)P^{(0)}
with
z^{(0)}_{\alpha,\beta}=p^{(0)}_{\alpha}y_{\alpha+\beta}p^{(0)}_{\beta}
.
Consequently,
y_{\alpha+\beta}=\frac{z^{(0)}_{\alpha,\beta}}{p^{(0)}_{\alpha}p^{(0)}_{\beta}}
.
By the definition of
\alpha_{\gamma}
and
\beta_{\gamma}
, we have
y_{\gamma}=\frac{z^{(0)}_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}}{p^{(0)}_{\alpha_{\gamma}}p^{(0)}_{\beta_{\gamma}}}
.
Moreover, for all
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
satisfying
\alpha\leq\beta
,
(\alpha,\beta)\neq(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})
, and
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
,

\frac{z^{(0)}_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}}{p_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{(0)}}=y_{\gamma}=\frac{z^{(0)}_{\alpha,\beta}}{p_{\alpha}^{(0)}p_{\beta}^{(0)}}\,.

(345)

Defining
\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}=\diag(\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,0)},0,\dots,0)
with
\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,0)}
as in (
326
), constraint (
345
) becomes

\tr(\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}Z)=\tr(\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,0)}Z^{(0)})=0\,.

(346)

The number of such
\bar{A}^{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}
, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}
satisfying
\alpha\leq\beta
,
(\alpha,\beta)\neq(\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma})
, and
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
,
\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
, is

\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]-\binom{n+2k}{n}
.

For
i=1,\dots,m
, the constraint
Z^{(i)}=P^{(i)}M_{k-d_{i}}(g_{i}y)P^{(i)}
is equivalent to
P^{(i)-1}Z^{(i)}P^{(i)-1}=M_{k-d_{i}}(g_{i}y)
.
For
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}
,
\alpha\leq\beta
, this leads to

\begin{array}[]{rl}\frac{z^{(i)}_{\alpha,\beta}}{p_{\alpha}^{(i)}p_{\beta}^{(i)}}=&\sum_{\eta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}}g_{i,\eta}y_{\alpha+\beta+\eta}\\
=&\sum_{\gamma\in\alpha+\beta+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}}g_{i,\gamma-\alpha-\beta}y_{\gamma}\\
=&\sum_{\gamma\in\alpha+\beta+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}}g_{i,\gamma-\alpha-\beta}\left(\frac{z^{(0)}_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}}{p_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{(0)}}\right)\\[10.0pt]
=&\sum_{\gamma\in\alpha+\beta+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}}\frac{g_{i,\gamma-\alpha-\beta}}{p_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{(0)}}z^{(0)}_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}\,.\end{array}

(347)

Introducing
\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta)}=\diag(\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,0)},0,\dots,0,\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,i)},0,\dots,0)
, where
\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,0)}
are defined as in (
327
)
and
\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,i)}
defined as in (
328
), the constraint (
347
) becomes

\tr(\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta)}Z)=\tr(\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,0)}Z^{(0)})+\tr(\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta,i)}Z^{(i)})=0\,.

(348)

The number of such
\bar{A}^{(i,\alpha,\beta)}
, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}
,
\alpha\leq\beta
,
i=1,\dots,m
, is

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{2}|{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i})}|(|{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i})}|+1)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}\left[\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}+1\right]\,.

(349)

For
j=1,\dots,l
, the constraint
M_{k-w_{j}}(h_{j}y)=0
is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{rl}\forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}\,,\,0=&L_{y}(x^{\alpha}h_{j})=L_{y}(x^{\alpha}\sum_{\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}h_{j,\beta}x^{\beta})\\
=&L_{y}(\sum_{\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}h_{j,\beta}x^{\alpha+\beta})\\
=&\sum_{\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}h_{j,\beta}y_{\alpha+\beta}\\
=&\sum_{\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}y_{\gamma}\\
=&\sum_{\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}\left(\frac{z^{(0)}_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}}{p_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{(0)}}\right)\\[10.0pt]
=&\sum_{\gamma\in\alpha+{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}}\frac{h_{j,\gamma-\alpha}}{p_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{(0)}}z^{(0)}_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}\,.\end{array}

(350)

Defining
\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha)}=\diag(\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha,0)},0,\dots,0)
, where
\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha,0)}
is as in (
329
),
the constraint (
350
) becomes

\tr(\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha)}Z)=\tr(\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha,0)}Z^{(0)})=0\,,\,\forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}\,.

(351)

The number of such
\bar{A}^{(j,\alpha)}
, for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}
,
j=1,\dots,l
, is

\sum_{j=1}^{l}|{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2(k-w_{j})}|=\sum_{j=1}^{l}\binom{n+2(k-w_{j})}{n}\,.

(352)

Additionally, we have

1=y_{0}=\frac{z_{0,0}^{(0)}}{p_{0}^{(0)}p_{0}^{(0)}}=\tr(\bar{A}^{(0,0)}Z^{(0)})=\tr(\bar{A}^{(0)}Z)\,,

(353)

where
\bar{A}^{(0)}=\diag(\bar{A}^{(0,0)},0,\dots,0)
, with
\bar{A}^{(0,0)}
as in (
330
).

The objective function of the problem (
322
) is expressed as

L_{y}(f)=\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}f_{\gamma}y_{\gamma}=\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}f_{\gamma}\frac{z^{(0)}_{\alpha_{\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}}}{p_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{(0)}}=\tr(C^{(0)}Z^{(0)})=\tr(CZ)\,,

(354)

where
\bar{C}=\diag(\bar{C}^{(0)},0,\dots,0)
, with
\bar{C}^{(0)}
as in (
331
).
Thus, (
322
) is equivalent to (
341
).

(
v
) follows, as the optimal value of (
322
) remains unchanged when the constraint
\tr(PD_{k}(y)P)\leq 1
is added or removed.

(
ii
) From the definition of
\bar{C}
, it follows that

\|\bar{C}\|_{F}\leq\max_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}\frac{2^{k+1}}{p_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{(0)}}\|f\|_{\ell_{2}}=\max_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}\frac{2^{k+1}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}\|f\|_{\ell_{2}}\leq 2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{2}}\,,

(355)

\|\text{vec}(\bar{C})\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq\max_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}\frac{2^{k+1}}{p_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{(0)}}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}=\max_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}\frac{2^{k+1}}{u_{\alpha_{\gamma}}^{1/2}u_{\beta_{\gamma}}^{1/2}}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq 2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\,.

(356)

(
iii
) follows directly from the definition of
\bar{N}
:
\bar{N}=\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}
.

(
iv
) Recall that the total number of affine constraints in the SDP (
341
) includes:

•

Constraints from
Z^{(0)}=P^{(0)}M_{k}(y)P^{(0)}
, which contribute

\frac{1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}+1\right]-\binom{n+2k}{n}\,.

(357)

•

Constraints from
Z^{(i)}=P^{(i)}M_{k-d_{i}}(g_{i}y)P^{(i)}
for
i=1,\dots,m
, which contribute

\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}\left[\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}+1\right]\,.

(358)

•

Constraints arising from
M_{k-w_{j}}(h_{j}y)=0
, for
j=1,\dots,l
, which contribute

\sum_{j=1}^{l}\binom{n+2(k-w_{j})}{n}\,.

(359)

•

A single constraint from
y_{0}=1
.

Summing these contributions gives the total number of affine constraints
\tilde{M}
.

∎

Lemma 56
.

Let
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2k}
and
g_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2d_{i}}
, for
i=1,\dots,m
,
h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2w_{j}}
, for
j=1,\dots,l
, with
g_{1}=1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
.
Assume
k\geq\max\{d_{1},\dots,d_{m},w_{1},\dots,w_{l}\}
.
Let
C
,
A_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,M
,
N
,
c
,
\lambda_{\min}
, and
\lambda_{\max}
be generated by Algorithm
9
.
Define

A=\begin{bmatrix}\text{vec}(A_{1})\\
\dots\\
\text{vec}(A_{M})\end{bmatrix}\,.

(360)

Set
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{\tau_{k}}{c}
.
Then, the following properties hold:

(i)

-I_{N}\preceq C\preceq I_{N}
, and
\|\text{vec}(C)\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq\frac{2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}}{c}
.

(ii)

A
has rank
M
,
\sigma_{\min}(A)=1
, and
-I_{N}\preceq A_{i}\preceq I_{N}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
.

(iii)

\lambda^{\star}\in[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
.

(iv)

Problem (
322
) is equivalent to (
50
).

(v)

\lambda^{\star}=\inf\{\tr(CX)\,:\,X\in\mathbb{S}^{N}_{+}\,,\,\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\}\,.

(361)

Moreover, if
y
is a feasible solution for problem (
322
), then for any
\omega\geq 0
,
\diag(PD_{k}(gy)P,\omega)
is a feasible solution for (
361
), where
P
is constructed as in Lemma
54
.

(vi)

c\leq 2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{2}}
.

(vii)

M\leq\frac{m+1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]+1
,
N\leq(m+1)\binom{n+k}{n}+1
, and

\tilde{M}\leq 1+(l-1)\binom{n+2k}{n}+\frac{m+1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]
.

(viii)

The maximum number of nonzero entries in a row of
C,A_{i}
is at most
s=\binom{n+k}{n}
.

Proof.

(
iv
) By Lemma
55
(
i
), the problem (
322
) is equivalent to (
341
).
Using the definitions of
\tilde{C},\tilde{A}_{i},\tilde{b}_{i}
, the problem (
341
) can be reformulated as:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau_{k}=\inf\limits_{X}&\tr(\tilde{C}X)\\
\text{s.t.}&X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{N}\,,\,\tr(X)=1\,,\\
&\tr(\tilde{A}_{i}X)=\tilde{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}\,.\end{array}

(362)

Applying Lemma
60
, the matrices
\check{A}_{1},\dots,\check{A}_{M}
are linearly independent in
\mathbb{S}^{N}
, and

\tr(\tilde{A}_{i}Z)=\tilde{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}\Leftrightarrow\tr(\check{A}_{i}Z)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\,.

(363)

Further, by Lemma
62
, the matrices
A_{1},\dots,A_{M}
satisfy
\tr(A_{i}A_{j})=\delta_{ij}
, and and the equivalence holds:

\tr(\check{A}_{i}X)=\check{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\Leftrightarrow\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M

(364)

This implies that problem (
362
) is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau_{k}=\inf\limits_{X}&\tr(\tilde{C}X)\\
\text{s.t.}&X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{N}\,,\,\tr(X)=1\,,\\
&\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\,.\end{array}

(365)

Rewriting this problem using
C=\frac{\tilde{C}}{c}
, we obtain:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\frac{\tau_{k}}{c}=\inf\limits_{X}&\tr(CX)\\
\text{s.t.}&X\in\mathbb{S}_{+}^{N}\,,\,\tr(X)=1\,,\\
&\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\,.\end{array}

(366)

With
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{\tau_{k}}{c}
, this problem is of the form (
50
).

(
v
) follows from Lemma
55
(
v
).

(
ii
) Since
\tr(A_{i}A_{j})=\delta_{ij}
,
-I_{N}\preceq A_{i}\preceq I_{N}
,
A
has rank
M
, and
\sigma_{\min}(A)=1
.

(
vi
) derives from the fact that
c=\|\tilde{C}\|_{F}=\|\bar{C}\|_{F}
(from Lemma
55
(
ii
)).

(
i
) Consequently, with
C=\frac{\tilde{C}}{c}=\frac{\tilde{C}}{\|\tilde{C}\|_{F}}
, it follows that
-I_{N}\preceq C\preceq I
and by Lemma
55
(
ii
),

\|\text{vec}(C)\|_{\ell_{1}}=\frac{\|\text{vec}(\tilde{C})\|_{\ell_{1}}}{c}=\frac{\|\text{vec}(\bar{C})\|_{\ell_{1}}}{c}\leq\frac{2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}}{c}\,.

(367)

(
iii
) Since
\lambda_{\max}=\frac{f(a)}{c}
for
a\in S(g)\cap V(h)
, and
\lambda_{\min}=\frac{\underline{\tau}}{c}
and
\underline{\tau}\leq\tau_{k}\leq f^{\star}\leq f(a)
, it follows that
\lambda^{\star}\in[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
.

(
vii
) is obtained from the bound
M\leq\min\{\tilde{M},L\}
with
N=\bar{N}+1
,
\tilde{M}=\bar{M}+1
,

L=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}\left[\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}+1\right]+1\,,

(368)

and Lemma
55
(
iii
) and (
iv
).

(
viii
) The diagonal-block structure of
C,A_{i}
yields (
viii
).
∎

Remark 13
.

The maximum number of nonzero entries in a row of

\tilde{C},\tilde{A}_{i}
is at most:

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\max\limits_{\begin{array}[]{cc}\scriptstyle i=1,\dots,m,\\
\scriptstyle j=1,\dots,l\\
\end{array}}\{|\supp(f)|,|\supp(g_{i})|,|\supp(h_{j})|\}\\
\leq&\max\limits_{\begin{array}[]{cc}\scriptstyle i=1,\dots,m,\\
\scriptstyle j=1,\dots,l\\
\end{array}}\{|{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d}|,|{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}|,|{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2w_{j}}|\}\\
=&\max\limits_{\begin{array}[]{cc}\scriptstyle i=1,\dots,m,\\
\scriptstyle j=1,\dots,l\\
\end{array}}\left\{\binom{n+2d}{n},\binom{n+2d_{i}}{n},\binom{n+2w_{j}}{n}\right\}\,.\end{array}

(369)

However, the application of Gaussian elimination and the Gram-Schmidt process transforms each
A_{i}
into a linear combination of the
\tilde{A}_{i}
matrices. As a result, the sparsity structure of
\tilde{A}_{i}
is generally lost.

Theorem 11
.

Let
f\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2k}
and
g_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2d_{i}}
, for
i=1,\dots,m
,
h_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}[x]_{2w_{j}}
, for
j=1,\dots,l
, with
g_{1}=1-\|x\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}
.
Assume
k\geq\max\{d,d_{1},\dots,d_{m},w_{1},\dots,w_{l}\}
,
\tau_{k}\geq 0
, and Slater’s condition holds for the moment relaxation (
322
), i.e., there exists a feasible solution
\bar{y}
for problem (
322
) such that
D_{k}(g\bar{y})\succ 0
and has condition number
\kappa
.
Let
\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}
be the value returned by Algorithm
9
.
Then, the following inequality holds:

\begin{array}[]{rl}0\leq&\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}-\tau_{k}\\
\leq&\varepsilon 2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa r_{g,n,k})\sqrt{\frac{m+1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]+1}\ \right]\,,\end{array}

(370)

where
r_{g,n,k}=\frac{\max\Lambda_{g,n,k}}{\min\Lambda_{g,n,k}}
with
\Lambda_{g,n,k}=\{p_{\alpha}^{(i)2}\,:\,i=0,\dots,m\,,\,\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}\}

and
p_{\alpha}^{(i)}
defined as in Step
1
a-d of Algorithm
9
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{\tau_{k}}{c}
. Since Slater’s condition holds for the moment relaxation (
322
), strong duality is satisfied for problem (
322
) and its dual.
By Lemma
52
(
vii
), strong duality also holds for problem (
361
) and its dual (
42
).

Define
U_{n,k}=\diag(PD_{k}(g\bar{y})P,\xi)
, where
P
is constructed as in Lemma
54
and
\xi=\lambda_{\min}(PD_{k}(g\bar{y})P)
. Since
P\succ 0
, it follows that
U_{n,k}\succ 0
and has condition number

\kappa(U_{n,k})=\kappa(PD_{k}(g\bar{y})P)\leq\kappa r_{g,n,k}\,.

(371)

By Lemma
56
(
v
),
U_{n,k}
is a feasible solution for problem (
361
), and thus Slater’s condition holds for problem (
361
).
From Lemma
56
(
ii
),
A
has full rank
M
.
Since
\tau_{k}\geq 0
, we have
\lambda^{\star}=\frac{\tau_{k}}{c}\geq 0
.
By Lemma
56
(
i
), (
ii
) and (
iii
),
I_{N}\succeq C\succeq-I_{N}
,
I_{N}\succeq A_{i}\succeq-I_{N}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
, and
\lambda^{\star}\in[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
.
Using Lemma
44
(ii), we obtain

0\leq\lambda^{\star}-\overline{\lambda}_{T}\leq\varepsilon\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa(U_{n,k}))\|\text{vec}(C)\|_{\ell_{1}}\sigma_{\min}(A)^{-1}\sqrt{M}\right]\,.

(372)

Applying properties of
C
and
A
from Lemma
56
(
i
) and (
ii
), this simplifies to

0\leq\lambda^{\star}-\overline{\lambda}_{T}\leq\varepsilon\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa r_{g,n,k})\frac{2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}}{c}\sqrt{M}\right]\,.

(373)

Since
\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}=\underline{\lambda}_{T}c
and
\tau_{k}=\lambda^{\star}c
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}0\leq\tau_{k}^{(\varepsilon)}-\tau_{k}=&c(\lambda^{\star}-\underline{\lambda}_{T})\\[5.0pt]
\leq&\varepsilon\left[2c+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa r_{g,n,k})2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\sqrt{M}\right]\\[10.0pt]
\leq&\varepsilon\left[2^{k+2}\|f\|_{\ell_{2}}+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa r_{g,n,k})2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\sqrt{M}\right]\\[10.0pt]
\leq&\varepsilon 2^{k+1}\|f\|_{\ell_{1}}\left[2+(1+\sqrt{2}\kappa r_{g,n,k})\sqrt{M}\right]\,.\end{array}

(374)

The third inequality is due to Lemma
56
(
vi
).
Using
M\leq\frac{m+1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]+1
, we conclude the result.
∎

Lemma 57

(Complexity)
.

Let
k\geq\max\{d,d_{1},\dots,d_{m},w_{1},\dots,w_{l}\}
.
The time complexity to run Algorithm
9
on a classical computer is:

O\left(m\binom{n+k}{n}^{2}\left[(l-1)\binom{n+2k}{n}+m^{2}\binom{n+k}{n}^{4}+m^{2}\binom{n+k}{n}^{2}\operatorname{poly}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\right]\right)\,,

(375)

while on a quantum computer, the time complexity is:

\footnotesize O\left(\binom{n+k}{n}^{1.5}\left\{m(l-1)\binom{n+k}{n}^{0.5}\binom{n+2k}{n}+m^{3}\binom{n+k}{n}^{4.5}+m^{0.5}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}^{0.5}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right]\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\right\}\right)\,.

(376)

Proof.

The most computationally intensive parts of Algorithm
9
include Gaussian elimination in Step
1o
, the Gram-Schmidt process in Step
1p
, and the binary search with Hamiltonian updates in Step
2
.

For Gaussian elimination, by Lemma
61
, both the classical and quantum computers have the same time complexity of
O(\tilde{M}L^{2})
, with

L=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{m}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}\left[\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}+1\right]+1\,.

(377)

This implies
L\leq\frac{m+1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]+1
.

For Gram-Schmidt process, by Lemma
63
, both classical and quantum computers have the same time complexity of
O(LM^{2})
.

Regarding the binary search with Hamiltonian updates, by Lemma, by Lemma
15
, the classical computer requires
O((MsN+N^{\omega})\varepsilon^{-2})
operations, and the quantum computer requires
O(s(\sqrt{M}+\sqrt{N}\varepsilon^{-1})\varepsilon^{-4})
operations, where
s
is the maximum number of nonzero entries in any row of the data matrices
C,A_{i}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
.
By Lemma
56
(
viii
), we have
s=\binom{n+k}{n}
.

Additionally, by Lemma
56
(
vii
), we know that
M\leq\frac{m+1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]+1
,
N\leq(m+1)\binom{n+k}{n}
and

\tilde{M}\leq 1+(l-1)\binom{n+2k}{n}+\frac{m+1}{2}\binom{n+k}{n}[\binom{n+k}{n}+1]
.
Thus, the result follows.
∎

Appendix C
Positive lower bounds for the smallest eigenvalues of moment and localizing matrices associated with a positive measure

Lemma 58
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{S}^{N}_{+}
.
Then

\lambda_{\min}(A)\geq\left(\frac{N-1}{\tr(A)}\right)^{N-1}\det(A)\,.

(378)

Proof.

Let
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{N}
be the eigenvalues of
A
, so that
\lambda_{\min}(A)=\lambda_{N}
.
Since
A\succeq 0
, we have
\lambda_{N}\geq 0
.
By the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality, this implies

\begin{array}[]{rl}\det(A)=\lambda_{1}\dots\lambda_{N-1}\lambda_{N}\leq&\left(\frac{\lambda_{1}+\dots+\lambda_{N-1}}{N-1}\right)^{N-1}\lambda_{N}\\[5.0pt]
\leq&\left(\frac{\lambda_{1}+\dots+\lambda_{N-1}+\lambda_{N}}{N-1}\right)^{N-1}\lambda_{N}\\[5.0pt]
=&\left(\frac{\tr(A)}{N-1}\right)^{N-1}\lambda_{N}\,.\end{array}

(379)

Hence the result follows.
∎

Lemma 59
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{Z}^{N\times N}
be a symmetric positive definite matrix (
A\succ 0
). Then
\det(A)\geq 1
.

Proof.

Let
A=(a_{ij})_{i,j=1,\dots,N}
. By definition, the determinant of
A
is expressed as:

\det(A)=\sum_{\sigma\in S^{N}}\sign(\sigma)a_{1\sigma(1)}\dots a_{N\sigma(N)}\,,

(380)

where
S^{N}
is the symmetric group of all bijections
\sigma:\{1,\dots,N\}\to\{1,\dots,N\}
, and
\sign(\sigma)
is the signature of
\sigma
, which equals
1
for even permutations and
-1
for odd permutations.
Since
a_{ij}\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
i,j
, it follows that
\det(A)\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Additionally, because
A\succ 0
, we know
\det(A)>0
. Combining these facts, we conclude
\det(A)\geq 1
.
∎

Proposition 1
.

Let
\mu
be the Lebesugue measure on
[0,1]^{n}
.
Assume that
n\geq 2
.
Then, the smallest eigenvalue of the moment matrix
M_{k}(\mu)
satisfies:

\lambda_{\min}(M_{k}(\mu))\geq[(2k+1)!]^{-n\binom{n+k}{n}}\,.

(381)

Proof.

Define
\nu=C_{k}\mu
for a constant
C_{k}>0
, and let
y=(y_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}
be the truncated moment sequence of
\nu
.
The moment matrix
M_{k}(\nu)
is given by
M_{k}(\nu)=M_{k}(y)=(y_{\alpha+\beta})_{\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}}
.

We first show that
M_{k}(\nu)\succ 0
.
Consider any
u\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\binom{n+k}{n}}\backslash\{0\}
.
Then,

u^{\top}M_{k}(\nu)u=\int u^{\top}v_{k}v_{k}^{\top}ud\nu=\int(u^{\top}v_{k})^{2}d\mu=C_{k}\int_{[0,1]^{n}}p^{2}dx\geq 0\,,

(382)

where
p=u^{\top}v_{k}

This implies that
M_{k}(\nu)\succeq 0
.
Suppose, for contradiction, that
u^{\top}M_{k}(\nu)u=0
. Then,
\int_{[0,1]^{n}}p^{2}dx=0

Since
p^{2}\geq 0
, this implies
p=0
on
[0,1]^{n}
, so that
p
is the zero polynomial. Thus,
u=0
, a contradiction. Hence,
M_{k}(\nu)\succ 0
.

Next, calculate
y_{\alpha}
:

\begin{array}[]{rl}y_{\alpha}=C_{k}\int_{[0,1]^{n}}x^{\alpha}dx=&C_{k}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{1}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}dx_{i}\\[10.0pt]
=&C_{k}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}+1}=\frac{C_{k}}{(\alpha_{1}+1)\dots(\alpha_{n}+1)}\,.\end{array}

(383)

To ensure
y_{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{N}}
for all
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
choose
C_{k}=[(2k+1)!]^{n}
. Since
\alpha_{i}+1\in\{1,\dots,2k+1\}
for
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}
,

\frac{C_{k}}{\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\alpha_{i}+1)}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(2k+1)!}{\alpha_{i}+1}\in{\mathbb{N}}\,.

(384)

By Lemma
59
,
\det(M_{k}(\nu))\geq 1
.
To estimate
\tr(M_{k}(\nu))
, note

\tr(M_{k}(\nu))=\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}}y_{2\alpha}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{2e_{i}}+\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k}\backslash\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}}y_{2\alpha}\,,

(385)

where
\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is the standard basis in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Then,

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tr(M_{k}(\nu))\leq&\frac{nC_{k}}{3}+C_{k}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}-n\right]\\[10.0pt]
=&C_{k}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}-\frac{2n}{3}\right]\\[10.0pt]
\leq&C_{k}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}-1\right]\,,\end{array}

(386)

where the last inequality holds because
n\geq 2
.
Using Lemma
58
,

\begin{array}[]{rl}C_{k}\lambda_{\min}(M_{k}(\mu))=&\lambda_{\min}(C_{k}M_{k}(\mu))=\lambda_{\min}(M_{k}(C_{k}\mu))\\[7.0pt]
=&\lambda_{\min}(M_{k}(\nu))\\[7.0pt]
\geq&\left(\frac{\binom{n+k}{n}-1}{\tr(M_{k}(\nu))}\right)^{\binom{n+k}{n}-1}\det(M_{k}(\nu))\,.\end{array}

(387)

Since
\det(M_{k}(\nu))\geq 1
and
\tr(M_{k}(\nu))\leq C_{k}\left[\binom{n+k}{n}-1\right]
,

\lambda_{\min}(M_{k}(\nu))\geq C_{k}^{-\binom{n+k}{n}+1}\,.

(388)

Thus,
\lambda_{\min}(M_{k}(\mu))\geq C_{k}^{-\binom{n+k}{n}}
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 14
.

Using the Stirling approximation
m!\sim m^{m}e^{-m}\sqrt{2\pi m}
, we have:

C_{k}=[(2k+1)!]^{n}\sim(2k+1)^{(2k+1)n}e^{-(2k+1)n}[2\pi(2k+1)]^{\frac{n}{2}}\,,

(389)

which grows rapidly as
n
and
k
increase.

To improve the bound, we can instead define
C_{k}
as the least common multiple (LCM):

L_{k,n}=\text{LCM}\left(\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\alpha_{i}+1)\,:\,\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}\right\}\right)\,.

(390)

Using this approach provides a tighter positive lower bound for
\lambda_{\min}(M_{k}(\mu))
.

For
n=1
,
L_{k,1}
is asymptotically equivalent to the exponential of the Chebyshev function for
k
.
However, the asymptotic behavior of
L_{k,n}
for
n\geq 2
remains unknown.

Let
\mathbb{K}[x]_{d}
denote the set of polynomials with coefficients in
\mathbb{K}
of degree at most
d
, where
\mathbb{K}
is
\mathbb{Z}
or
\mathbb{Q}
.

Proposition 2
.

Let
\mu
be the Lebesugue measure on
[0,1]^{n}
, and let
g_{i}\in\mathbb{Q}[x]_{2d_{i}}
, for
i=0,\dots,m
, such that
g_{i}>0
on
[0,1]^{n}
, for
i=1,\dots,m
.
Let
t_{i}
be the smallest positive integer such that
t_{i}g_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}[x]_{2d_{i}}
.
Then,

\lambda_{\min}(D_{k}(\mu g))\geq\frac{1}{[(2k+1)!]^{n}}\min_{i=0,\dots,m}\frac{1}{t_{i}}\left(\frac{\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}-1}{[(2k+1)!]^{n}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}t_{i}\|g_{i}\|_{\ell_{1}}}\right)^{\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}-1}\,.

(391)

Proof.

Define
\nu=C_{k}\mu
with
C_{k}=[(2k+1)!]^{n}
.
Let
y=(y_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2k}}
denote the truncated moment sequence of
\nu
.
Define
\tilde{g}_{i}=t_{i}g_{i}
.
Since
\tilde{g}_{i}=\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}}g_{i,\gamma}x^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{Z}[x]_{2d_{i}}
and
y\subset\mathbb{Z}
(as shown in the proof of Proposition
1
), it follows that

\begin{array}[]{rl}M_{k-d_{i}}(\tilde{g}_{i}\nu)=M_{k-d_{i}}(\tilde{g}_{i}y)=&\left(\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}}t_{i}g_{i,\gamma}y_{\alpha+\beta+\gamma}\right)_{\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}\\[10.0pt]
&\in\mathbb{Z}^{\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}\times\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}}\,.\end{array}

(392)

We claim that

M_{k-d_{i}}(\tilde{g}_{i}\nu)\succ 0
.
Indeed, we take
w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}}\backslash\{0\}
.
Since
\tilde{g}_{i}=t_{i}g_{i}>0
on
[0,1]^{n}
,

\begin{array}[]{rl}w^{\top}M_{k-d_{i}}(\tilde{g}_{i}\nu)w=&w^{\top}\left(\int\tilde{g}_{i}v_{k-d_{i}}v_{k-d_{i}}^{\top}d\nu\right)w\\[10.0pt]
=&\int\tilde{g}_{i}w^{\top}v_{k-d_{i}}v_{k-d_{i}}^{\top}wd\nu\\[10.0pt]
=&C_{k}\int_{[0,1]^{n}}\tilde{g}_{i}p^{2}dx\geq 0\,,\end{array}

(393)

where
p=w^{\top}v_{k-d_{i}}
.
This implies
M_{k-d_{i}}(\tilde{g}_{i}\nu)\succeq 0
.
Assume by contradiction that
0=w^{\top}M_{k-d_{i}}(\tilde{g}_{i}\nu)w
. Then,

0=w^{\top}\left(\int\tilde{g}_{i}v_{k-d_{i}}v_{k-d_{i}}^{\top}d\nu\right)w=\int\tilde{g}_{i}w^{\top}v_{k-d_{i}}v_{k-d_{i}}^{\top}wd\nu=C_{k}\int_{[0,1]^{n}}\tilde{g}_{i}p^{2}dx\,.

(394)

It implies that
\tilde{g}_{i}p^{2}=0
on
[0,1]^{n}
, which gives
p=0
on
[0,1]^{n}
since
\tilde{g}_{i}=t_{i}g_{i}>0
on
[0,1]^{n}
. Thus,
p
is zero polynomial, which contradicts
w\neq 0
.
By Lemma
59
,
\det(M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu\tilde{g}_{i}))\geq 1
.
To bound
\tr(M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu\tilde{g}_{i}))
, note:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tr(M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu\tilde{g}_{i}))=&\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}}t_{i}g_{i,\gamma}y_{2\alpha+\gamma}\\[7.0pt]
\leq&\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}t_{i}\sum_{\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{2d_{i}}}|g_{i,\gamma}|y_{2\alpha+\gamma}\\[7.0pt]
\leq&C_{k}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}_{k-d_{i}}}t_{i}\|g_{i}\|_{\ell_{1}}\\[7.0pt]
\leq&C_{k}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}t_{i}\|g_{i}\|_{\ell_{1}}\,.\end{array}

(395)

The second inequality follows from
0\leq y_{2\alpha+\gamma}\leq C_{k}
.

Next, we estimate a lower bound for
\lambda_{\min}(M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu g_{i}))
.
Using Lemma
58
,

\begin{array}[]{rl}\lambda_{\min}(M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu g_{i}))=&\lambda_{\min}(M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu\frac{1}{t_{i}}\tilde{g}_{i}))\\
=&\lambda_{\min}(\frac{1}{t_{i}}M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu\tilde{g}_{i}))\\
=&\frac{1}{t_{i}}\lambda_{\min}(M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu\tilde{g}_{i}))\\
\geq&\frac{1}{t_{i}}\left(\frac{\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}-1}{\tr(M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu\tilde{g}_{i}))}\right)^{\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}-1}\\
\geq&\frac{1}{t_{i}}\left(\frac{\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}-1}{[(2k+1)!]^{n}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}t_{i}\|g_{i}\|_{\ell_{1}}}\right)^{\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}-1}\,.\end{array}

(396)

Let us find a positive lower bound for
\lambda_{\min}(D_{k}(\mu g))
.
Since

D_{k}(\nu g)=\diag((M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu g_{i}))_{i=0}^{m})\,,

(397)

we have

\begin{array}[]{rl}C_{k}\lambda_{\min}(D_{k}(\mu g))=&\lambda_{\min}(C_{k}D_{k}(\mu g))=\lambda_{\min}(D_{k}((C_{k}\mu)g))\\[10.0pt]
=&\lambda_{\min}(D_{k}(\nu g))=\min\limits_{i=0,\dots,m}\lambda_{\min}(M_{k-d_{i}}(\nu g_{i}))\\[10.0pt]
\geq&\min\limits_{i=0,\dots,m}\frac{1}{t_{i}}\left(\frac{\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}-1}{[(2k+1)!]^{n}\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}t_{i}\|g_{i}\|_{\ell_{1}}}\right)^{\binom{n+k-d_{i}}{n}-1}\,,\end{array}

(398)

yielding the result.
∎

Appendix D
Basic algorithms

D.1
Gaussian elimination

Algorithm 10
.

Gaussian elimination

•

Input:
\tilde{A}_{i}\in\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}^{N_{j}}
,
\tilde{b}_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, for
i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}
.

•

Output:
\check{A}_{i}\in\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}^{N_{j}}
,
\check{b}_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
.

1.

Convert each
\tilde{A}_{i}
into a row vector:

a_{i}=\begin{bmatrix}a_{11}&a_{12}&\dots&a_{1L}\end{bmatrix}=\text{Vec}(\tilde{A}_{i})^{\top}\,,\,b_{i}=\tilde{b}_{i}\,,\text{ for }i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}\,.

(399)

2.

Construct the augmented matrix

[A|b]
by concatenating the rows of

a_{i}
and
b_{i}
:

[A|b]=\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
a_{2}&b_{2}\\
\dots&\dots\\
a_{\tilde{M}}&b_{\tilde{M}}\\
\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}a_{11}&a_{12}&\dots&a_{1L}&b_{1}\\
a_{21}&a_{22}&\dots&a_{2L}&b_{2}\\
.&.&\dots&.&.\\
a_{\tilde{M}1}&a_{\tilde{M}2}&\dots&a_{\tilde{M}L}&b_{\tilde{M}}\\
\end{bmatrix}\,.

(400)

3.

Initialize
m=0
and
l=0
.

4.

Increment
m
by one unit.

5.

Increment
l
by one unit.

6.

If
l>L
, stop the algorithm. Otherwise, proceed to the next step.

7.

If
a_{il}=0
, for all
i=m,\dots,\tilde{M}
, go back to Step 5. Otherwise, proceed to the next step.

8.

Interchange the
m
-th row with any row
i>m
in
[A|b]
such that
a_{il}\neq 0
(if
i=m
there is no need to perform an interchange).

9.

For
i=m+1,\dots,\tilde{M}
, add
-a_{il}/a_{ml}
times the
m
-th row to the
i
-th row of the augmented matrix
[A|b]
.

10.

If
m<\tilde{M}-1
, return to Step 4.
Otherwise, proceed to the next step.

11.

Set

M=\max\{i\in\{1,\dots,\tilde{M}\}\,:\,\begin{bmatrix}a_{i}&b_{i}\end{bmatrix}\neq 0\}\,.

(401)

Then, extract the matrices:
\check{A}_{i}=\text{Mat}(a_{i})
,
\check{b}_{i}=b_{i}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
.

Lemma 60
.

Let
\tilde{A}_{i}\in\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}^{N_{j}}
,
\tilde{b}_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}
, such that there exists
\bar{X}\in\mathbb{S}^{N}
with
N=\sum_{i=1}^{r}N_{i}
satisfying
\tr(\tilde{A}_{i}\bar{X})=\tilde{b}_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}
.
Let
\check{A}_{i}\in\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}^{N_{j}}
,
\check{b}_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
i=1,\dots,M
, be the outputs of Algorithm
10
.
Then, the set
\check{A}_{1},\dots,\check{A}_{M}
is linearly independent in
\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}^{N_{j}}
, with
M\leq\min\{\tilde{M},L\}
, where
L=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{r}N_{i}(N_{i}+1)
, and the equivalence

\tr(\check{A}_{i}X)=\check{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\Leftrightarrow\tr(\tilde{A}_{i}X)=\tilde{b}_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,\tilde{M}

(402)

holds.

Lemma 61
.

To execute Algorithm
10
, both classical and quantum computers exhibit the same complexity of
O(\tilde{M}L^{2})
, where
L=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{r}N_{i}(N_{i}+1)
.

D.2
Gram-Schmidt process

Algorithm 11
.

Gram-Schmidt process

•

Input:
\check{A}_{i}\in\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}^{N_{j}}
,
\check{b}_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
.

•

Output:
A_{i}\in\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}^{N_{j}}
,
b_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
.

1.

Initialize the first pair as
\hat{A}_{1}=\check{A}_{1}
,
\hat{b}_{1}=\check{b}_{1}
,
A_{1}=\frac{\hat{A}_{1}}{\|\hat{A}_{1}\|_{F}}
,
b_{1}=\frac{\hat{b}_{1}}{\|\hat{A}_{1}\|_{F}}
.

2.

For
j=2,\dots,M
:

(a)

Orthogonalize
\hat{A}_{j}
by subtracting the projections onto the previous
\hat{A}_{i}
s

\hat{A}_{j}=\check{A}_{j}-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\frac{\tr(\check{A}_{j}\hat{A}_{i})}{\|\hat{A}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}}\hat{A}_{i}\,.

(403)

(b)

Adjust the corresponding
\hat{b}_{j}
by the same projections:

\hat{b}_{j}=\check{b}_{j}-\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\frac{\tr(\check{A}_{j}\hat{A}_{i})}{\|\hat{A}_{i}\|_{F}^{2}}\hat{b}_{i}\,.

(404)

(c)

Normalize the result:
A_{j}=\frac{\hat{A}_{j}}{\|\hat{A}_{i}\|_{F}}
,
b_{j}=\frac{\hat{b}_{j}}{\|\hat{A}_{j}\|_{F}}
.

Lemma 62
.

Let
\check{A}_{i}\in\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}^{N_{j}}
and
\check{b}_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
, such that
\check{A}_{1},\dots,\check{A}_{M}
are linearly independent in
\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}^{N_{j}}
.
Let
A_{i}\in\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}^{N_{j}}
and
b_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, for
i=1,\dots,M
be matrices and real numbers returned by Algorithm
11
.
Then,
\tr(A_{i}A_{j})=\delta_{ij}
, and we have equivalent

\tr(\check{A}_{i}X)=\check{b}_{i}\,,\,\text{for }i=1,\dots,M\Leftrightarrow\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,\text{for }i=1,\dots,M\,.

(405)

Lemma 63
.

To execute Algorithm
11
, both classical computer and quantum computer have the same complexity of
O(LM^{2})
, where
L=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{r}N_{i}(N_{i}+1)
.

Appendix E
Useful lemmas

Lemma 64
.

Let
a,b,c\in(0,\infty)
. Define square matrix of size
n+1

W=\begin{bmatrix}1&a&a&\dots&a\\
a&b&c&\dots&c\\
a&c&b&\dots&c\\
.&.&.&\dots&.\\
a&c&c&\dots&b\end{bmatrix}\,.

(406)

Then
\det(W-\lambda I_{n+1})=(b-c-\lambda)^{n-1}[(1-\lambda)(b-c-\lambda)-n(a^{2}-(1-\lambda)c)]
.

Proof.

We have

\begin{array}[]{rl}\det(W-\lambda I_{n+1})=\begin{vmatrix}1-\lambda&a&a&\dots&a\\
a&b-\lambda&c&\dots&c\\
a&c&b-\lambda&\dots&c\\
.&.&.&\dots&.\\
a&c&c&\dots&b-\lambda\end{vmatrix}\end{array}

(407)

Subtracting the
j
-th column from the last column,
j=2,\dots,n
, we get

\begin{array}[]{rl}\det(W-\lambda I_{n+1})=\begin{vmatrix}1-\lambda&0&0&\dots&0&a\\
a&b-c-\lambda&0&\dots&0&c\\
a&0&b-c-\lambda&\dots&0&c\\
.&.&.&\dots&.&.\\
a&0&0&\dots&b-c-\lambda&c\\
a&c-b+\lambda&c-b+\lambda&\dots&c-b+\lambda&b-\lambda\\
\end{vmatrix}\end{array}

(408)

Multiplying the first row by
\frac{c}{a}
, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{rl}\det(W-\lambda I_{n+1})=\frac{a}{c}\begin{vmatrix}\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}&0&0&\dots&0&c\\
a&b-c-\lambda&0&\dots&0&c\\
a&0&b-c-\lambda&\dots&0&c\\
.&.&.&\dots&.&.\\
a&0&0&\dots&b-c-\lambda&c\\
a&c-b+\lambda&c-b+\lambda&\dots&c-b+\lambda&b-\lambda\\
\end{vmatrix}\end{array}

(409)

Subtracting the
j
-th row from the first row,
j=2,\dots,n
, we get

\begin{array}[]{rl}\det(W-\lambda I_{n+1})=\frac{a}{c}\begin{vmatrix}\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}&0&0&\dots&0&c\\
a-\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}&b-c-\lambda&0&\dots&0&0\\
a-\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}&0&b-c-\lambda&\dots&0&0\\
.&.&.&\dots&.&.\\
a-\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}&0&0&\dots&b-c-\lambda&0\\
a-\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}&c-b+\lambda&c-b+\lambda&\dots&c-b+\lambda&b-c-\lambda\\
\end{vmatrix}\end{array}

(410)

Expanding the determinant with respect to the first row, we get

\begin{array}[]{rl}\det(W-\lambda I_{n+1})=&\frac{a}{c}\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}\begin{vmatrix}b-c-\lambda&0&\dots&0&0\\
0&b-c-\lambda&\dots&0&0\\
.&.&\dots&.&.\\
0&0&\dots&b-c-\lambda&0\\
c-b+\lambda&c-b+\lambda&\dots&c-b+\lambda&b-c-\lambda\\
\end{vmatrix}\\[30.0pt]
&+\frac{a}{c}(-1)^{n}c\begin{vmatrix}a-\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}&b-c-\lambda&0&\dots&0\\
a-\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}&0&b-c-\lambda&\dots&0\\
.&.&.&\dots&.\\
a-\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}&0&0&\dots&b-c-\lambda\\
a-\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}&c-b+\lambda&c-b+\lambda&\dots&c-b+\lambda\\
\end{vmatrix}\\[30.0pt]
=&(1-\lambda)(b-c-\lambda)^{n}-(-1)^{n}a[a-\frac{(1-\lambda)c}{a}](b-c-\lambda)^{n-1}T\\
=&(1-\lambda)(b-c-\lambda)^{n}-(-1)^{n}[a^{2}-(1-\lambda)c](b-c-\lambda)^{n-1}T\,,\end{array}

(411)

where

T=\begin{vmatrix}-1&1&0&\dots&0\\
-1&0&1&\dots&0\\
.&.&.&\dots&.\\
-1&0&0&\dots&1\\
-1&-1&-1&\dots&-1\\
\end{vmatrix}\,.

(412)

To compute
T
, adding to the
j
-th column the sum of the first
j-1
columns,
j=2,\dots,n
, we get

T=\begin{vmatrix}-1&0&0&\dots&0&0\\
-1&-1&0&\dots&0&0\\
-1&-1&-1&\dots&0&0\\
.&.&.&\dots&.&.\\
-1&-1&-1&\dots&-1&0\\
-1&-2&-3&\dots&-(n-1)&-n\\
\end{vmatrix}=(-1)^{n}n\,.

(413)

From this, the result follows.
∎

Lemma 65
.

Consider semidefinite program:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\lambda^{\star}=\inf\limits_{X}&\tr(CX)\\
\text{s.t.}&X\in\mathbb{S}^{N}_{+}\,,\,\tr(A_{i}X)=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\,.\end{array}

(414)

The dual of (
414
) is given by:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau^{\star}=\sup\limits_{\xi,Z}&b^{\top}\xi\\
\text{s.t.}&\xi\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M}\,,\,Z\in\mathbb{S}^{N}_{+}\,,\,\sum_{i=1}^{M}\xi_{i}A_{i}+Z=C\,.\end{array}

(415)

Assume that strong duality holds for problems (
414
)-(
415
). Let
(X^{(\varepsilon)},\xi^{(\varepsilon)},Z^{(\varepsilon)})
be an
\varepsilon
-optimal solution returned by the interior-point methods in
[
25
]
and
[
4
]
.
Then
\lambda^{\star}=\tau^{\star}
and

\begin{cases}\lambda^{\star}\leq\tr(CX^{(\varepsilon)})\leq\lambda^{\star}+\varepsilon N\,,\\
\tau^{\star}-\varepsilon N\leq b^{\top}\xi^{(\varepsilon)}\leq\tau^{\star}\,.\end{cases}

(416)

Proof.

By the strong duality theorem,
\lambda^{\star}=\tau^{\star}
.
From
[
4
, Section 7.3]
, we know that:

\begin{cases}X^{(\varepsilon)}\succ 0\,,\,\tr(A_{i}X^{(\varepsilon)})=b_{i}\,,\,i=1,\dots,M\,,\\[5.0pt]
Z^{(\varepsilon)}\succ 0\,,\,\sum_{i=1}^{M}\xi_{i}^{(\varepsilon)}A_{i}+Z^{(\varepsilon)}=C\,,\\[5.0pt]
\frac{\tr(X^{(\varepsilon)}Z^{(\varepsilon)})}{N}\leq\varepsilon\,.\end{cases}

(417)

Thus,
X_{\varepsilon}
is a feasible solution to (
414
) and
(\xi_{\varepsilon},Z_{\varepsilon})
is a feasible solution to (
415
).
This implies:

\begin{array}[]{rl}\tau^{\star}=\lambda^{\star}\leq&\tr(CX^{(\varepsilon)})=\tr((\sum_{i=1}^{M}\xi^{(\varepsilon)}_{i}A_{i}+Z^{(\varepsilon)})X^{(\varepsilon)})\\[5.0pt]
\leq&\sum_{i=1}^{M}\xi^{(\varepsilon)}_{i}\tr(A_{i}X)+\tr(Z_{\varepsilon}X_{\varepsilon})\\[5.0pt]
\leq&\sum_{i=1}^{M}\xi^{(\varepsilon)}_{i}b_{i}+\varepsilon N\\[5.0pt]
=&b^{\top}\xi^{(\varepsilon)}+\varepsilon N\\
\leq&\tau^{\star}+\varepsilon N=\lambda^{\star}+\varepsilon N\,.\end{array}

(418)

Hence, the desired inequalities hold.
∎

Appendix F
Block encoding constructions for
B^{(\gamma)}

Recall
B^{(\gamma)}\in\mathbb{S}^{\binom{n+k}{n}}
from Definition
7
, whose entries satisfy

(B^{(\gamma)})_{\alpha,\beta}=1
iff
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
(componentwise) for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}_{k}^{n}
, and
0
otherwise.
We now give a simple “projected–unitary” (normalization
1
) block–encoding that exploits the fact that

\alpha\mapsto\gamma-\alpha
is an involution on its valid domain.

Proposition 3

(Projected–unitary block–encoding)
.

Fix
\gamma\in{\mathbb{N}}_{2k}^{n}
.
There exists a unitary
U_{\gamma}
acting on three registers

\mathcal{H}_{A}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{W}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{b}
,
where
A
stores an index
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}_{k}^{n}
,

W
is a work register of the same shape (initialised to
|0\rangle
),
and
b
is a one–qubit flag (initialised to
|1\rangle
),
such that with the projector

\Pi:=I_{A}\otimes|0\rangle\!\langle 0|_{W}\otimes|1\rangle\!\langle 1|_{b}

we have

\Pi\,U_{\gamma}\,\Pi\;=\;B^{(\gamma)}\otimes|0\rangle\!\langle 0|_{W}\otimes|1\rangle\!\langle 1|_{b}\,.

Consequently,
U_{\gamma}
is a
1
-block–encoding of
B^{(\gamma)}
.
Moreover,
U_{\gamma}
uses
O(n\log k)
reversible arithmetic gates and one controlled–SWAP.

Proof.

On computational basis states
|\alpha\rangle_{A}|0\rangle_{W}|1\rangle_{b}
with
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}_{k}^{n}
,
define
U_{\gamma}
by the following reversible steps:

(1)

Compute
\beta
and validity.
Reversibly compute the componentwise difference

\beta:=\gamma-\alpha
into
W
.
Perform a controlled not on
b
controlled on all components of
\beta
being nonnegative and
|\beta|\leq k
(otherwise leave
b=0
).

(2)

Controlled swap.
Controlled on
b=1
, swap the contents of
A
and
W
.

(3)

Uncompute work.
Recompute
\gamma-\alpha
into
W
(where after the swap
A=\beta
on the valid branch), which resets
W
to
|0\rangle
.

All three steps are reversible; (3) restores the work register, so
U_{\gamma}
is unitary.

If
\alpha
is
invalid
, then after (1) the flag is
b=0
, the controlled swap does not fire, and uncomputation restores
W=0
. Thus

U_{\gamma}|\alpha,0,1\rangle=|\alpha,0,0\rangle
,
which is orthogonal to the range of
\Pi
, hence contributes zero to the compressed block.

If
\alpha
is
valid
, then
\beta=\gamma-\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}_{k}^{n}
.
After (1) we have
|\alpha,\beta,1\rangle
; the controlled swap yields
|\beta,\alpha,1\rangle
;
the uncompute step maps
W:\ \alpha\mapsto\alpha-(\gamma-\beta)=0
.
Therefore

U_{\gamma}|\alpha,0,1\rangle=|\beta,0,1\rangle

with
\beta=\gamma-\alpha
.
Projecting in/out with
\Pi
produces exactly the transition amplitude

\langle\beta|\,B^{(\gamma)}\,|\alpha\rangle=1

when
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
, and
0
otherwise.
Hence
\Pi U_{\gamma}\Pi=B^{(\gamma)}\otimes|0\rangle\!\langle 0|_{W}\otimes|1\rangle\!\langle 1|_{b}
.
The gate/space bounds follow from implementing componentwise add/subtract and comparisons on
n
registers of size
O(\log k)
, and one controlled–SWAP on those registers.
∎

Thus, we see that we can efficiently construct the block encodings required by our algorithm in the unconstrained case.

Extension to
B^{(\gamma)}_{g}
.

For constrained POPs we use the block–diagonal matrices

B^{(\gamma)}_{g}=\diag\!\big(\sum_{\zeta}g_{i,\zeta}\,B^{(\gamma-\zeta)}_{i}\big)_{i=0}^{m}

(see (
117
)).
Selecting the block
i
and applying a linear combination of unitaries (LCU) over the at most
s_{g}
nonzero coefficients
\{g_{i,\zeta}\}
,
with the above
U_{\gamma-\zeta}
as primitives,
gives a block–encoding of
B^{(\gamma)}_{g}
.
Under the rescaling assumption
\sum_{\zeta}|g_{i,\zeta}|\leq 1
for each
i
(Assumption
3
),
the LCU normalization remains
1
.
The per–application cost is
O(s_{g})
invocations of the
U_{\gamma-\zeta}
gadgets plus
O(\log s_{g})
overhead for state preparation/selection. Thus, all we need is access to the an auxilliary state with amplitudes
\{g_{i,\zeta}\}
to produce the relevant block encodings for constrained POP.
